Phase 3 — Automation & Skills (✓ complete 2026-06-18)

Skills run themselves on schedules and events; the model router goes live with cost-aware routing; skills are tested for regressions; and run-outcome data becomes visible and actionable. Waves 3-6 and 3-7 are the Phase-1 deferrals, re-homed here.

  • 3-1 — Skill/Hook/Workflow Registry. skills + skill_runs tables, skills.ts, REST routes, SkillsPage, cron scheduler + event listener, boundary validation. Done — CRUD plus manual / schedule / event triggers dispatch runs; invalid cron or trigger-field mismatch is rejected with 400 at the API boundary.
  • 3-2 — Ollama provider + cost-aware routing. Replaced the ollamaProvider stub with a real ollama run provider; extended route() with preferLocal/maxCostUsd hints, mean completed-claude-cost run-outcome data, and a background reachability probe. Done — route() returns claude unless ENABLE_OLLAMA AND a probe confirms reachability; an unreachable/absent Ollama degrades to claude (warn, never fails a run); supervisor unchanged (it dispatches + parses on the routed provider, so an ollama run can't run/parse as claude).
  • 3-3 — Skill testing via eval-harness. skill_evals table; runSkillTest dispatches a supervised eval run (eval-harness methodology, EVAL VERDICT marker); POST /api/skills/:id/test
    • GET …/evals. Done — each test records pass/fail; a regression (was-pass, now-fail vs the prior baseline) is flagged + badged on SkillsPage; a non-dispatchable eval degrades to a durable failed eval.
  • 3-4 — Routing improvement dashboard. Pure aggregateRouting over windowed runs (cost / latency / success by provider+model — there is no task taxonomy on runs, so provider+model is the routing dimension); GET /api/metrics/routing; RoutingPage with an outcome table, a cost-by-model trend (reusing the stacked-SVG chart), a plain-language recommendation, and an empty state. Done.
  • 3-6 — Web terminal (re-homed from Phase 1). A node-pty shell bridged over a new /ws/terminal WS route, rendered with xterm.js. Done — default-OFF (ENABLE_TERMINAL); auth-guarded by a scoped TERMINAL_TOKEN (distinct from HARNESS_TOKEN, the only token in the web bundle); node-pty dynamically imported so a missing binding degrades to a clean error, never crashing boot; session disposed on disconnect. Verified live (real echo on a valid token; no shell spawned on a bad token).
  • 3-7 — Structured task/goal records + GitHub Issues sync (re-homed from Phase 1). GitHubProvider.syncIssues reconciles gh issue list into project_tasks (insert / close / reopen / no-clobber of in_progress); Tasks-tab sync button. Done — mapping verified with a mocked gh; absent gh (or a no-remote project) degrades to { synced: 0, degraded: true } at 200, matching the PR/CI poller posture.
  • 3-5 — Phase 3 close-out. These sections + roadmap progress finalized, lessons captured, whole-implementation review (no blockers; in-scope fixes landed) + full verification (typecheck · core 345 / web 81 tests · build) green, merged to main. Live headless-Chromium pass across all 10 routes caught and fixed a blank-screen regression — the react-force-graph aggregate threw AFRAME is not defined at module-eval time, blanking every route; switched to the 2D-only react-force-graph-2d subpackage (guarded by a static import test).

Phase H — Knowledge Graph Engine & Experience Polish

Every registered project gets a live, navigable, actionable code graph; the interface feels finished (hybrid glass + motion); the bible self-demonstrates via a real UI artifact. One reviewable commit per wave (implementer → spec-review → quality-review → controller → CI).

Phase H is experience-focused and lettered like Phase G; it slots in before Phase 4 (originally Multi-Device).

  • H-1 — Graph build engine (core). project_graphs table (status/built_at/last_commit/node+edge counts/error); buildGraph() runs npx gitnexus analyze via an injected seam with an in-flight guard + EventBus emit; POST /api/projects/:id/graph/build + status/stale on GET …/graph; transient graph_update WS; shared graph-status schema; graph.test.ts (CI never invokes real GitNexus).
  • H-2 — Enrichment, dispatch, auto-reindex (core). Live node enrichment in GET …/graph (last-touched run, verify findings, bible — bible scoped to the harness project only); POST /api/projects/:id/graph/dispatch (node-scoped run via startRun, single-line input guard → 400); auto-reindex via an onRunUpdate subscriber (per-project debounced, guarded; env flag GRAPH_AUTO_REINDEX, default on).
  • H-3 — Knowledge Graph UI. Build/Refresh button + building spinner + last-built/stale/error chips + WS-driven auto-refresh; node inspector enriched facts + enabled Dispatch Agent (confirm-card → dispatch → transient notice); graph polish (status coloring, legend, spring physics, center/zoom on click, reduced-motion); fleet-graph polish (nodes-only, no invented edges).
  • H-4 — Hybrid glass + motion. Glass tokens + backdrop-blur utilities + @supports fallback (index.css); glass applied to hero surfaces only (command bar, modals, node inspector, activity strip); centralized lib/motion.ts variants + MotionConfig reduced-motion; 60fps check.
  • H-5 — UI artifact system. core/src/ui-artifact.ts (compileUiArtifact writes rich HTML to disk verbatim + upserts md); POST /api/ui-artifact/compile; seeded harness ui-demo (interactive mini Command Deck, hybrid-glass); DocViewer UI badge/link; ui-artifact.test.ts (preserves interactive HTML, output-path isolation).
  • H-6 — create-web-ui-artifact skill + bible docs. New .claude/skills/create-web-ui-artifact/SKILL.md (UI counterpart to onboarding), seeded into the skills registry as a manual workflow; bible §08 (hybrid glass + motion + graph engine + UI artifact), §09 (this Phase H), §10 (D-009/D-010/D-011); skill-registration + bible-isolation tests.
  • H-7 — Verify, CI, whole-implementation review. verify-project / health audit; pnpm typecheck && pnpm -r test && pnpm build + CI green on the branch; whole-implementation review across all waves; recompile bible; merge --no-ff.

Todo delegation workflow (✓ delivered 2026-06-24)

The Tasks tab can multi-select todos and run the harness delegation loop over the selection as one supervised orchestrator run. Decision D-012.

  • workflows.ts seam: buildDelegationPrompt (pure) + dispatchTaskWorkflow lifecycle (lock tasks → insert workflow_runs → startRun → finalize on terminal run, with degrade-on-throw) + deriveWorkflowStatus / finalizeWorkflowRun + typed TaskNotFoundError.
  • workflow_runs table + (project_id, created_at) index + workflowRunsDb helpers (db.ts).
  • POST /api/projects/:id/tasks/dispatch (body { taskIds }, 1..50) → 202 { workflowRunId, runId }; 404 unknown project; 400 invalid body / foreign taskId (TaskNotFoundError); 500 otherwise. Web api api.projects.tasks.dispatchWorkflow.
  • Tasks-tab multi-select (per-row checkboxes, indeterminate select-all), sticky Run delegation workflow action bar, in_progress-on-dispatch (never auto-done), success toast with "View run →".

Follow-on: staged, per-stage visible/retryable execution (Idea 2) — a workflow_stages table with dispatchTaskWorkflow spawning one startRun per stage chained on eventBus (shared branch threaded across stages); the route, api client, and UI stay unchanged (D-012).

One reviewable commit per wave (implementer → spec-review → quality-review → controller → CI); a whole-effort review + live Playwright smoke before merge.

The originally-planned "Phase 4 — Multi-Device" was re-scoped: the desktop/mobile surfaces are deferred (see below, D-019) and this phase delivered the agent-interaction UX (Track A) and observability (Track D) instead.

  • Wave V — whole-effort verification. Gates green (typecheck · core/web tests · build), whole-effort pre-merge review (MERGE-READY; in-scope review-fixes landed incl. context_tokens persistence round-trip), live Playwright smoke; merged --no-ff to main.

Deferred to a later phase (D-019): the Tauri desktop app (bundled-core sidecar, tray, native notifications) — old Track B; full spec retained in ~/.claude/plans/read-through-and-analyze-rippling-hanrahan.md. Also deferred: PWA mobile (installable + push), further remote-access hardening (reverse proxy / Tailscale), and passkey/TOTP auth hardening (the Phase-1 → Phase-4 re-homing above — not shipped in the re-scoped Phase 4).

Update (2026-07-10) — the desktop app is now DELIVERED, re-scoped from Tauri to Electron. The bundled-core / tray / native-notification desktop app deferred above shipped as a Windows Electron app in a new desktop/ workspace: core runs as a Node-20 child, the SPA is served same-origin, the shell auto-authenticates, and it installs via NSIS with unsigned auto-update from public GitHub Releases. See §15 Desktop App and D-089 → D-094. (PWA mobile, cross-platform builds, code-signing, and passkey/TOTP auth hardening remain deferred.)

Extracted the 3×-duplicated supervised-run lifecycle into run-lifecycle.ts::trackSupervisedRun (the seam startAgentRun extends in 5.1); de-duplicated the path-guard / escHtml / TERMINAL-set / row-mapper utilities; removed dead parser params. Phase 5 — Agentic Org (✓ 2026-07-02 — the Agentic Org delivered on feat/k-org; --no-ff merge to main at close-out)

Re-frame the product from "an operator drives a dashboard" to "the user directs an agent organization" (§03, §04): K the friendly secretary → the Chief manager → staff-engineer orchestrator leads → workflow definitions → role subagents. All three durable tiers are one AgentProfile entity gated by an authority tier, enforced by tier-scoped MCP servers + the --allowedTools allowlist. Memory starts at layer A (file lessons + gated reflection). Decisions D-020 → D-025 (+ D-030 / D-031 for local-model management & voice). One reviewable commit per wave (delegation loop + review every wave).

5.1 — Foundation + K

  • AgentProfile entity (tier · charter · defaultModel · allowedTools · mcpServers · skills) + storage
  • startAgentRun(profileId, { trigger, goal|thread, projectId?, workflowId? }) generalizing startRun
  • Authority gating: --allowedTools allowlist per tier (coding tools at lead tier only)
  • logistics-mcp (K) — shipped (D-039). Honesty note (2026-07-02): the second half of this box over-claimed — the Google Calendar / Gmail / Drive connectors are NOT wired into K (they remain operator-side only; K's tier mounts kstore + logistics and nothing else — §03). Wiring the real connectors stays an open follow-up
  • Memory layer A: per-profile markdown lessons + gated end-of-run reflection (operator-approved)
  • K runtime (hybrid): durable thread + warm interactive session + fresh-seeded run on restart/idle/wake — runtime redesigned (2026-07-04, D-062): the warm/fresh hybrid became a resumable one-shot (a stable per-thread --session-id/--resume CLI session, answer-and-exit, no park); the durable thread stays the source of truth
  • agent_tasks (K-owned global checklists) distinct from project_tasks — delivered as the OPPOSITE design (2026-07-02 annotation): there is no agent_tasks table and no split — the as-built model is the unified work_items store with scopes (run/personal/org/project, D-026 → D-048 → D-053); project_tasks itself was later dropped (D-058). The capability the box wanted (K-owned durable checklists) ships as the personal/org scopes
  • K-home dashboard surface (the friendly landing)

5.2 — Chief

  • Chief profile (chief tier) + mgmt-mcp (assign-lead / pick-workflow / scope-projects / report)
  • Reuse GitNexus MCP read-only for the Chief
  • Autonomous wake via the Phase-3 scheduler + event listener (schedule/event triggers)
  • K → Chief delegation (engineering work only) + report-back up the chain
  • Chief dashboard surface (assignments board, wake history, read-only control-plane mirror)

5.3 — Roster + Workflows

  • Orchestrator leads (Frontend · Backend · Systems · Security · Network) as profiles + charters
  • Status-write MCP for leads; per-lead skills/tools/MCP scoping (the control plane)
  • WorkflowDefinition generalizing buildDelegationPrompt; implement+review as the first definition
  • Cross-project scope flag in the schema (multi-project execution deferred)
  • Orchestrator-detail surface: charter/skills/tools/MCP/memory editors + persistent authority panel + live delegation tree
  • Settings org / MCP authority-tier panel

5.4 — Voice

Talk to your agents instead of typing. v1 is push-to-talk in via a new TranscriptionProvider B-seam + a local Whisper service; voice-out (TTS) is a later wave. Decision D-031.

  • Push-to-talk in (v1). TranscriptionProvider B-seam (transcribe(audio)→{text}) + a default local Whisper (faster-whisper) impl; POST /api/transcribe (core proxies — browser holds no key); ENABLE_VOICE / WHISPER_BASE_URL config; graceful degrade to keyboard when unreachable
  • Mic in every composer. useVoiceRecorder (MediaRecorder) + a MicButton wired into the K composer, the ⌘K bar, and the HITL reply box; transcript inserts as ordinary text into the existing dispatch / reply flow
  • Voice status card in Settings (engine reachable · model · enable toggle)
  • Voice out (TTS — K talks back) — deferred to a later wave (un-checked 2026-07-02: this line was marked done while saying "deferred"; nothing TTS has shipped)

5.5 — Local model management (extends the ModelRouter seam — independent of the org tiers)

See and shape the local models behind the router from the UI: discover, download, and select — with the selection applied live. Decision D-030.

  • app_config store + route() getters → the active Ollama model is operator-selectable and hot-swappable, no restart
  • Model surface over the Ollama HTTP API: list installed (/api/tags), pull with live progress on the EventBus→WS wire (/api/pull), remove (/api/delete)
  • Curated catalog (sizes) + fs.statfs disk-fit check; advanced free-form "pull any tag"
  • Settings → Local models surface (installed · catalog · active selector · live download progress); dynamic Ollama models in the dispatch picker

5.6 — Close-out (✓ 2026-07-02)

Multi-tier org observability, the whole-org SEAMS review, docs + decision-log finalize, and the --no-ff merge to main.

  • Autonomous execution loop (D-049 → D-051) — split into a (dispatch intent + wake wiring), b1 (the MAIN-process lead-dispatch-relay decoupling: dispatch_lead records a pending intent in the mgmt-server child, the main process CAS-claims + executes it so the lead's agent_runs finalizes and the lead→Chief report-back fires on the main EventBus), and b2 (Chief→K continuation
    • the multi-tier org DelegationTree render).
  • Multi-tier org observability — the per-run sub-agent tree generalized to the whole-org tree (fullOrgToDelegationTree over the tagged AgentEvent stream + agent_runs/k_thread_turns links; the K→Chief edge derived from kDelegations), §13.
  • Whole-org fresh-eyes SEAMS review across the full feat/k-org diff — 7 seams PASS, 0 blocker/major, 1 minor fixed pre-merge (the lead charter referenced a non-existent github MCP tool; corrected to gh pr create over the granted Bash path). Verdict: MERGE-WITH-FOLLOWUPS.
  • Full verification green from a clean tree — typecheck · core 1317 / web 407 · build; CI green on feat/k-org.
  • Bible §03/§04/§08/§13 + this roadmap + decision log (D-020 → D-052) finalized; lessons captured.

Live smokes passed (each an operator-approved narrow real dispatch): P5.0 startAgentRun reaches terminal + finalizes once; "talk to K" routes a request with the route shown inline; the autonomous-loop relay drains a Chief-recorded intent → a real lead run runs, finalizes (agent_runs→completed), links the assignment, and reports back up the chain.

Delivered via splits (each its own reviewable wave): 5.1 → a–f (K profile · logistics-mcp · memory-A · hybrid runtime · task-model scope + the work_items storage collapse · K-home UI); 5.2 → a/b (Chief + mgmt-mcp + wake · Chief org-status UI + the reusable DelegationTree); 5.3 → a/b (roster + WorkflowDefinition + control plane · Orchestrators/Workflows UI + editors).

Deferred (post-merge follow-ups, non-breaking): the P5.1d2b task-store reroute (drop project_tasks; move routes/projects.ts / syncIssues / Tasks-UI off the compat shim; the work_item_create scope='project' guard + the updateProjectTaskFromIssue project_id bind); the full K→Chief→lead→PR end-to-end live run (operator-gated token spend — the relay mechanic is already smoke-proven); and the low-severity review nits (hardcoded operator name; grant-guard substring→typed error class; concatAssistantText unbounded read; kDelegations counts failed). (The d2b reroute and every listed nit were delivered in 5.7 below; the live PR run stays token-gated.)

5.7 — Post-merge fix delivery (✓ 2026-07-02)

A deep whole-system review of the merged Phase-5 org surfaced honesty gaps (cosmetic authority editors, ignored project scoping, frozen model seeds, run-scoped stores that should be durable, the d2b debt, unguarded wake spend, and UI drift from the approved demo). Six waves on feat/k-fixes closed them — every wave conductor-reviewed, all integrated CI-green.

  • C1 — web bug fixes. K-home no longer auto-navigates on send (the 5 s undo toast stays in place with a View run → link; a second send restarts the countdown); dialog focus traps + ⌘K a11y; live run_update WS invalidation of chief-org / orchestrators queries (throttled 250 ms); real TopBar breadcrumbs (Orchestrators › name, Workflows › name, Projects › name); shared runs-query key module; visible error states on K-home; --on-accent token; .worktrees/ gitignored.
  • B1 — authority enforcement + project-scoped dispatch + runtime model (D-054/D-055/D-056). synthesizeConfigDir honors the per-profile authority rows within the tier CEILING, fail-closed validate-before-mutate; the relay resolves scope_projects → the projects registry and passes projectId+cwd so leads run in the scoped repo; default_model un-frozen to the '' "runtime default" sentinel + one-shot migration; per-ask model override wins over profile over runtime.
  • A1 — durable operator-global stores (D-053). work_items scope 'run'|'personal'|'org'| 'project' (new 'run' = the old run-isolated semantics, kstore default); durable personal/org + the GET/POST/PATCH /api/k/work-items surface; one-shot table rebuild re-stamping legacy personal AND org rows to 'run'; logistics de-run-scoped; mgmt assignment_list/report_list read tools; agent_memory.profile_id populated + backfilled.
  • A2 — d2b completion (D-058). project_tasks DROPPED after a final one-shot backfill (kills the boot-resurrection of deleted tasks); projectTasksDb→projectWorkItemsDb as the first-class project surface (zero HTTP shape change); partial UNIQUE (project_id, issue_number) index + dedupe; updateProjectTaskFromIssue binds project_id; poller pathMissing degrade.
  • B2 — hygiene + wake governor (D-057/D-059). Org-relevance filter + rolling-hour cap (chief_wake_max_per_hour, default 6; suppressed wakes create no rows) + chief_wake_events_enabled kill switch on event wakes; K routing logistics-precedence; bounded assistant-text reads + report caps; isPathWithin resolves both sides (fixes the mixed-separator dispatch brick); typed GrantError; migrate() user_version fast path; stranded-thread boot sweep.
  • C2 — UI pragmatic parity (+ D-060). K-home real durable personal work items + Notes + Schedule cards (GET /api/k/notes//api/k/schedule) + composer power controls (model override + forced route); Chief actuation (hand-work composer, operator reassign PATCH /api/chief/assignments/:id, tree inspector Open-lead / View-run / Stop-run); per-lead recent-health lines + effectiveModel chip + authority add-affordances; the "Run this workflow" launcher (dispatch accepts workflowId, scaffold swap) + workflow-filtered run-tree picker; plus the dispatch-retirement liveness fix (D-060) unwedging re-dispatch/reassign.

Gates. Full fresh-tree verification after every wave; final: typecheck · core 1317 → 1463 passed (1 skipped) · web 407 → 457 passed · build, CI green on feat/k-fixes.

Conductor-review catches (each fixed in-wave, none shipped broken): the relay's post-claim strand window (a scope-read throw left a claimed intent 'dispatched' forever → degrade-to-failed inside the try); the legacy-org escalation (the A1 re-stamp originally covered only personal — an untouched legacy org row would have silently entered the durable operator view); the version-gate ordering hazard (stamp only after a successful scan; second-migrate tests reset user_version so slow-path idempotency stays under test); and the dispatch-retirement wedge (one successful dispatch permanently blocked re-dispatch and reassign — D-060).

Deferred / consciously not built: isPathWithin Windows case-insensitivity (two casings of one path can still compare unequal — fail-safe: it rejects, never escapes); the MemoryPage double-fetch (filter options derive from a second lessons read); mixed-intent messages under-escalate by design (a logistics keyword keeps an engineering ask with K — the cheap failure direction, D-057); the demo's Interactive checkbox for K sends dropped (it maps to nothing — the K path is already interactive by design); and the demo's health scores/bands, tier radios, and per-lead hues consciously not built (pragmatic parity: real derived health lines shipped instead of invented numbers).

K Expansion — Phase 5: Autonomy (✓ 2026-07-13 — feat/exp-p5, schema v11→v12)

The K Expansion Program's autonomy phase (E-14..E-18, E-27): the org can generate its own work, pull it, retry its own failures, cap its own spend, and derive its own lessons — all behind one persisted, default-OFF operator choice (Settings → Autonomous Org, D-107..D-109). Decisions D-107..D-113. One reviewable commit per lane; a whole-phase SEAMS review before merge.

  • Front door — Autonomous Org (D-107..D-109). CHIEF_WAKE → a persisted app_config (autonomy.settings) choice: master enabled (default OFF) + sub-toggles + max-concurrency + org budget cap + warn %, on a new SettingsAutonomy.tsx section; the env is deprecated warn-only; the schedulers always wire + gate per-tick so enabling ON needs no restart; Agents→Org shows a read-only status chip (§03, §08).
  • Master gate (D-108). autonomySettings().enabled gates chief auto-wake + proposals + backlog auto-pull + self-heal; the budget governor is the always-on exception (a safety cap, applies even when autonomy is OFF once set).
  • E-14 proposals. Deterministic ZERO-TOKEN collectors (ci_failed / verify_finding / open_issue / stale_bible) on a 15m cron → blocked org work_items with source/source_key, deduped + open-capped; Inbox proposal cards approve→open / dismiss→cancelled (sticky). Honest one-shot-per- project + no-undo limitations documented (D-111; §04, §07, §08).
  • E-15 backlog auto-pull. An interval relay CAS-claims the oldest open org item (open→in_progress), governed by the budget gate + max-concurrency; dispatches under the default orchestrator (§04).
  • E-17 budget governor. Reactive caps on MEASURED runs.cost_usd over a rolling 24h window — ZERO forecasting; org + per-project caps; at cap a dispatch is PARKED (429/BudgetCapError), no queue. Interactive/persistent K exempt; operator-action routes a tracked follow-up (D-112; §13).
  • E-18 self-heal. Deterministic failure classification off the last error event → retry with a fallback model (retry_count<2, budget headroom, original cwd, retry_of lineage, run_retried) or PARK an Inbox proposal with a one-line diagnosis; killed/interrupted skipped (§07).
  • E-27 eval-derived lessons. Repeated (≥2) same-signature failures → ONE deduped, capped pending agent_memory lesson through the existing D-041 gate (no new UI); off verify_results only, eval_results deferred (D-113; §04 memory layer C, §07).
  • E-16 routines first-class. The Automations tab (Agents→Skills) gains an NL→cron helper (rules-only, 400 on unmappable), next-run display, and measured cost per routine — no new table, no new rail slot (D-110; §08).
  • Insights → Charts. Budget burn-down (measured 24h) + retry-rate charts (§13).
  • Schema v11→v12. work_items.source/source_key (+ partial-unique idx); runs.retry_of/ retry_count/failure_class; projects.budget_daily_usd; the autonomy.settings blob.

Deferred / honest limits (default-OFF, tracked): project-keyed proposal source_keys are one-shot per project (a genuine recurrence isn't re-surfaced until the row clears) and inbox dismiss has no undo (D-111); the budget park does not yet gate operator-initiated action routes (rewind / review-fix / deep-verify / workflows / run-skill-now — D-112); E-27 covers verify_results only (eval_results has no failure-reason column — D-113).

Phase 6 — Intelligence & Scale (optional)

  • EventBus → NATS/Redis Streams + worker processes
  • Analytics on run data for routing improvement (memory layer B: structured store + outcome-weighted retrieval)
  • Verification/eval-derived lessons (memory layer C)
  • Knowledge-graph-driven agent context (GitNexus → agent prompts)

stable updated 2026-07-13

Architectural decisions with reasoning, newest last. Agents: append, never rewrite history; superseded decisions get a superseded-by note rather than deletion.

ID Date Decision Choice Why Rejected
D-001 2026-06-10 System architecture Monolith core with B-seams (EventBus, ModelRouter) Fastest path to a working system for one operator; seams make worker split / alt models a transport swap, not a rewrite Microservices day one (overhead); pure monolith without seams (paints into corner)
D-002 2026-06-10 Bible source of truth Compiled bible — structured md sections + live DB data → rendered HTML Agents edit small diffable sections, never raw HTML; visual quality is owned by the compiler so it never degrades; live data keeps docs honest Hand-authored HTML (agent edits erode it); flat markdown (doesn't scale, no live data)
D-003 2026-06-10 GitHub connection gh CLI + polling, SQLite cache, GitHubProvider seam Zero new infrastructure, already authenticated, works behind any network; 15–60s lag is acceptable for a single operator GitHub App + webhooks (public endpoint + app management is heavy for self-host)
D-004 2026-06-10 CI/CD verification Two-layer: GitHub Actions deterministic CI + agent-team verification skill Machines gate merges deterministically and run while the operator's machine is off; agents add judgment (coverage gaps, reviews, doc freshness) and repair the CI itself Local-only verification (invisible on GitHub); agents-as-CI (slow, token cost per push)
D-005 2026-06-10 Project model Registry, both onboarding paths (register local path / clone GitHub URL) Existing work registers in place; new work clones into the managed workspace; registry row is identical either way Managed-workspace-only (forces moving repos); path-only (harness should own cloning)
D-006 2026-06-10 Dashboard IA (IA superseded-by D-024) Command Deck: icon sidebar + ⌘K bar + swappable stage + activity strip; per-project workspace with 7 tabs incl. its own knowledge graph One consistent frame at every scope; graph is first-class at both fleet and project level without being the only navigation; mockups approved in visual review Card-grid home (graph buried); graph-first constellation (everything else one level down)
D-007 2026-06-10 Design language Precision minimal — near-black, hairline borders, single indigo accent, mono numerals, 150ms motion Daily-driver tool: ages well, renders fast, makes data the hero; density without noise Aurora glass (GPU cost, fights density); sci-fi HUD (gimmick fatigue)

D-009 2026-06-20 Design language layer Hybrid glass extends D-007 — keep precision-minimal for density/data, add a thin translucent backdrop-filter layer to hero surfaces only (command bar, modals, node inspector, activity strip), with an @supports opaque fallback and a MotionConfig reduced-motion default Adds depth/polish so the tool reads as finished without sacrificing the density and legibility D-007 buys; glass confined to non-data surfaces so it never fights data; @supports fallback prevents transparent-over-text on browsers lacking backdrop-filter Full aurora glass everywhere (the GPU-cost / density fight D-007 originally rejected); staying flat (interface read as unfinished); per-component ad-hoc motion (drift from the §06 motion tokens)
D-010 2026-06-20 Knowledge graph engine GitNexus-orchestrated, server-owned graph engine — project_graphs state table; buildGraph() shells npx gitnexus analyze behind an injected seam (in-flight guard, EventBus/WS emit); live node enrichment, node-scoped dispatch, and debounced auto-reindex on run completion One operator-triggerable, observable build pipeline with status/stale tracking; the analyze seam keeps GitNexus out of CI and unit tests deterministic; enrichment/dispatch/auto-reindex make the graph an actionable diagnostic surface, not a static picture Bundling a graph parser into core (re-implements GitNexus, drifts); building synchronously in the request (long analyze blocks the handler); auto-reindex with no debounce/guard (rebuild storms on busy projects)
D-011 2026-06-20 UI demo as a first-class artifact Compile the interactive UI demo to a stored artifact + ship a create-web-ui-artifact generator skill — rich self-contained HTML written to disk verbatim (bypassing the artifact sanitizer), served in a sandboxed iframe (allow-scripts, no allow-same-origin); the compile route accepts only { projectId }; a skill (modeled on onboarding) authors per-project demos The bible self-demonstrates the design language with a real, interactive surface; verbatim-on-disk keeps inline CSS/JS intact while the no-same-origin sandbox + projectId-only route keep untrusted HTML out of the verbatim-write path; the skill makes per-project demos repeatable Re-sanitizing the demo (strips the inline JS/CSS that make it interactive); accepting caller HTML at the route (opens a verbatim-write XSS path); a static screenshot (not interactive, drifts from the live design)
D-012 2026-06-24 Todo delegation-workflow execution model Idea 1 — one supervised orchestrator run per selection (workflows.ts + workflow_runs): a multi-select of todos dispatches a single controller run that runs the delegation loop (implementer → spec-review → quality-review → controller) in its own worktree and opens one PR; the workflows.ts/workflow_runs boundary preserves the Idea-2 option without paying for it The delegation loop is a prose methodology, not code, and startRun is strictly one-agent/one-worktree — so a selection naturally maps to one combined run that the agent decomposes internally; the seam + table keep the staged-engine option open (add a workflow_stages table, spawn one run per stage on eventBus) without building it now; the route, api client, and UI are unchanged when it grows Idea 2 — staged multi-run engine (requires threading one shared worktree/branch across independent runs, which the worktree-per-run model doesn't support: a large, risky subsystem); Idea 3 — workflow-as-skill (the loop is methodology/prose, not a discrete skill artifact, and would still bottom out on a single startRun)
D-013 2026-06-24 Vivid midnight-glass redesign + IA cleanup (IA superseded-by D-024) Re-skin to a vivid midnight-purple / blush-pink / sky-blue liquid-glass language and fix the navigation IA — extends D-009 with bolder glass, roundedness (18/14/10px radii) and generous spacing; the accent splits in two — blush #ff8fc0 for fills/active pills/badges, sky #38bdf8 for hover/active/focus transitions; a labeled collapsible sidebar replaces the 52px icon rail (Docs dropped as a top-level destination, Help/Settings → footer); the project-workspace "Bible" tab becomes "Artifacts" (a project-scoped artifact gallery); graph nodes gain glow + labels and edges become visible; hard-delete lands for projects (transactional, FK-safe) and tasks The precision-minimal indigo tool (D-007) read as an internal utility — dense, tiny icon-rail, invisible graph edges, no delete, a misnamed Bible tab. Product-quality polish (Apple-landing-page roundedness/depth), an intuitive labeled nav, a legible diagnostic graph, and delete affordances make it read as a product without sacrificing the density D-007 buys: status colours still carry meaning, glass stays hero-only, mono numerals stay. Supersedes the palette/IA of D-007/D-009; retains their density discipline + hero-only-glass principles Staying precision-minimal indigo (read as unfinished/internal); aurora-glass-everywhere (GPU cost + density fight D-007/D-009 reject); white text on the light blush accent (fails WCAG AA — accent FILLS use dark --bg text, accent-as-text stays on dark surfaces)
D-014 2026-06-27 Interactive multi-turn HITL Persistent-stdin interactive runs (A3.0 smoke-confirmed) — keep the child's stdin open; each operator turn is one {type:"user",message:{role,content}} line; {type:"result"} marks the turn boundary → park the run at the non-terminal awaiting_input; POST /api/runs/:id/input uses an atomic conditional UPDATE to guard the double-send race; /end closes stdin → done A real multi-turn conversation needs the same process + context + cost state across turns; the result line is the only reliable turn boundary on the stream-json wire; the conditional UPDATE makes the awaiting→running flip race-safe under double submits Re-spawn the CLI per turn (loses context/cost); kill on first result (no multi-turn); a non-atomic optimistic transition (double-send race)
D-015 2026-06-27 Event-data enrichment foundation Persist structured tool fields on each event (toolUseId/toolKind/toolInput/toolResult/toolResultIsError/subagentType/childLabel) parsed in parseClaudeLine; pair tool_use (assistant) ↔ tool_result (user) by tool_use_id across SEPARATE events; race-tolerant ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN migration; classifyTool table-maps a tool name → command/file/delegate/other The rich console + workflow tree need structured data, not raw re-parsed on every render; tool_use and its result arrive as separate stream-json events so a stored join key is required; a tolerant migration keeps multi-process/CI boots safe Parse raw JSON on the client each render (slow, fragile); collapse tool_use + result into one event (the wire delivers them separately)
D-016 2026-06-27 Workflow visualization Purpose-built CSS/SVG static defined-loop diagram from a shared DELEGATION_WORKFLOW constant + a live runtime sub-agent tree derived from delegate tool calls (delegated agents run IN-PROCESS via the Agent/Task tool, so a run's sub-agents ARE its delegate tool calls) The data is a small fixed hierarchy plus a per-run shallow tree, so a hand-laid diagram is simpler and crash-free; one shared constant keeps client + server in agreement without a new endpoint; the live tree reuses the D3/D4 pairing helpers A force-graph (wrong tool for a small fixed hierarchy + WebGL/AFRAME crash risk); a new GET endpoint for static data (a shared import suffices)
D-017 2026-06-27 Editable prompts & config Editable skill prompts via PATCH (partial update, name-collision → 409, clear→NULL) + a Settings page with provider/auth STATUS cards (claude/ollama/github/auth posture, NO secrets) and a guarded global CLAUDE.md editor (fixed path, gitnexus block preserved, atomic temp+rename, rolling backups, confirm-before-save, schema-locked body) The prompts + config that drive agents should be observable and editable in-app; status reveals provider/auth posture without leaking tokens; the editor is guarded so it can't traverse, drop the gitnexus block, or half-write a file A free-form path param for the editor (path traversal); per-project prompt editing now (global-only is the smaller, safer first step); returning the token value in status (leak)
D-018 2026-06-27 Context indicator + smoke-gated compaction Context-pressure indicator (ctx X / Y · Z%, warn ≥70 / danger ≥90) counting the FULL input context (fresh input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens, persisted as context_tokens so reloaded runs stay accurate) + real CLI /compact (a manual "Compact context" button AND a guarded/debounced hysteresis auto-compact). The D6.0 live smoke OVERTURNED the planned premise — /compact IS honored over the stream-json input wire (the CLI runs its compaction machinery), so on-demand compaction CAN be forced via sendInput('/compact') Fresh input_tokens alone underreports true context on cached runs; the smoke proved compaction is forceable, so a real Compact button + hysteresis auto-trigger beats a soft self-summary; persisting context_tokens keeps the meter honest after a reload The soft "summarize & continue" fallback (unnecessary once the smoke proved real /compact); counting fresh input_tokens only (underreports). Deferred: compact-and-CONTINUE success on a long non-empty context (a Wave V live item)
D-019 2026-06-27 Desktop app deferred Defer the Tauri desktop app (bundled-core sidecar, tray, native notifications) to a later phase; the web Command Deck stays the surface The observability + agent-UX priorities of this phase deliver more operator value per unit effort than re-packaging the existing web app; the full desktop spec is retained for later (~/.claude/plans/read-through-and-analyze-rippling-hanrahan.md) Building Tauri now (cost vs. this phase's observability / UX priorities)
D-020 2026-06-27 Agent-organization model Re-frame to a directed agent ORG with 3 authority tiers as ONE profile entity — K (secretary, the friendly face, no code), Chief (manager, delegates, no code), orchestrator leads (staff engineers, full coding within a charter); all are one AgentProfile differentiated by tier: secretary|chief|orchestrator; a lead's discipline (Frontend/Backend/Systems/Security/Network) is a capability bundle + charter, NOT a directory The product value is "you direct an org," not "you operate a dashboard"; one entity + a tier field keeps the model small while the tier gates capability; discipline-as-charter lets the roster grow by adding profiles, not code paths; matches how a real eng org delegates down and reports up Three separate agent codebases (triples the surface); discipline-by-directory (false constraint — a lead isn't bound to a folder); a flat single-agent assistant (no delegation, no specialization, no authority gating)
D-021 2026-06-27 Authority control plane Tier-scoped MCP servers + the --allowedTools allowlist enforce authority — logistics-mcp (K), mgmt-mcp (Chief), a status-write MCP (leads); coding tools (Bash/Write/Edit/Agent) only at the lead tier; reuse the Google Calendar/Gmail/Drive connectors (K) and GitNexus MCP read-only (Chief). It is a gating LAYER, not a fourth seam — events/models/GitHub still flow over the existing B-seams Authority must be enforced, not advisory — a tier physically cannot mount a server or call a tool it isn't granted, even if prompted; MCP scoping + the allowlist compose to do that; reusing existing connectors avoids rebuilding calendar/mail/code-intel; keeping it a gating layer preserves the B-seam architecture Prompt-only "please don't" guardrails (bypassable); one mega-MCP for all tiers (no isolation); a new transport seam for authority (over-engineered — gating composes onto the existing seams)
D-022 2026-06-27 Memory model Layered memory, START AT A — per-profile markdown lessons + a gated end-of-run reflection (the run proposes a pending lesson; the operator approves before it enters memory), composing with the project's lessons.md; storage shaped to grow into B (structured store + relevance retrieval + outcome-weighting, the ModelRouter posture) and C (verification/eval-derived lessons) Start simple and human-gated so nothing self-modifies its brain unreviewed; file lessons already exist, so A is mostly reuse; shaping the store + approval gate + outcome metadata now makes B/C a swap, not a rewrite (same grow-into-it posture the ModelRouter took for routing) A heavyweight vector store on day one (premature, unproven retrieval value); ungated self-editing memory (a bad lesson poisons the profile); no memory at all (the org never gets smarter)
D-023 2026-06-27 K runtime (K's warm-session hybrid superseded-by D-062) Persistent identity, ephemeral execution — every tier is durable (charter + memory + thread) but only ever RUNS as a bounded startAgentRun(profileId, { trigger, … }) activation; triggers = user-message (HITL), schedule/event (Phase-3 scheduler, reused), delegation (tier→tier). K specifically is HYBRID: the durable thread + memory is source of truth, a warm interactive session is kept while actively chatting, and a fresh seeded run is spun on restart/idle-timeout/scheduled wake A durable secretary you "talk to" can't be one never-ending process (cost/leak/restart-fragility), but re-seeding every message loses the feel of continuity; making identity durable and execution bounded gives both — continuity from stored thread+memory, robustness from short activations; reuses the supervisor/EventBus and the Phase-3 scheduler unchanged A single always-on K process (leaks, can't survive restart, unbounded cost); stateless re-seed per message (loses conversational continuity); a bespoke runtime outside the supervisor (re-implements what startRun already does)
D-024 2026-06-27 Redesign — evolve-visual + agent-first IA Evolve (not replace) vivid midnight-glass + reorganize the IA into Direct / Observe — keep #1a0f2e / #ff8fc0 / hero-only glass / mono numerals, warmed/friendlier on K-home; Direct group (K-home landing · Chief · Orchestrators + detail · Workflows + detail · Projects) + Observe group (Runs · Graph · Metrics · Routing · Terminal) + Settings/Help footer; the MCP/authority-tier panel is a persistent first-class panel on Orchestrator-detail (read-only mirror on Chief); the abstract Workflows diagram folds into Orchestrator/Workflow detail; confirm-card + toast-with-link is universal. Marks the IA of D-006 and D-013 superseded-by: D-024, retaining their density/glass/token discipline The org re-frame (D-020) makes an operator-centric tool IA wrong — the home should be the conversation with K and the nav should mirror the org; evolving the proven visual language (vs. a fresh re-skin) keeps the token discipline that already works; making the authority panel first-class makes the control plane legible; universal confirm + toast-with-link fixes the "hunt for your result" / silent-action gaps A from-scratch visual redesign (throws away working D-013 tokens); keeping the flat operator IA (contradicts the org model); a separate standalone Workflows diagram (floats without context — better embedded where a lead runs it)
D-025 2026-06-27 Roadmap re-baseline Phase 5 = Agentic Org (5.1 Foundation+K · 5.2 Chief · 5.3 Roster+Workflows · 5.4 Voice) becomes the headline next program; the old "Phase 5 — Intelligence & Scale" stub renumbers to Phase 6 (and absorbs memory layers B/C). Phase 4 (Agent-UX + Observability) is merged The agent org is now the central thesis (D-020), so it earns the next-phase headline; Intelligence & Scale (NATS/Redis, learned routing, graph-driven context) is real but optional and naturally follows the org, so it moves to Phase 6; memory B/C map cleanly onto it Slotting the org work as a sub-phase (under-sells the central re-frame); leaving Intelligence & Scale as Phase 5 (collides with the new headline); dropping the Intelligence stub (loses the committed scale work)
D-026 2026-06-27 IA simplification (Phase-5 UX) One front door · one org-status home · compose-is-confirm + undo · unified work-items · de-duplicated views — K (⌘K) is the only dispatch surface and every per-screen ⚡ is a SCOPED PREFILL of the K composer (to this lead/project/symbol), not an independent dispatch; K routes (logistics it handles, the Chief, or a specific lead) and shows the route INLINE before send. Chief is the SINGLE org-status home (objectives + one delegation tree + a thin health line, no full health strip). Send fires with a 5 s UNDO toast; a full confirm-card appears ONLY on escalation (T3 authority / cross-project / destructive kill·delete·reassign). ONE DelegationTree component reused at scopes (Chief whole-org · Orchestrator-detail one-lead); Graph 3D stays the structural code/fleet graph. UNIFIED work_items (scope: personal/org/project) replaces the agent_tasks/project_tasks split; every metric printed in exactly one place. Refines D-024. Many co-equal dispatch surfaces and duplicated org views read as cosmetic hierarchy and force a hunt-for-result; collapsing to one front door, one status home, compose-is-confirm + undo, and de-duplicated metrics/trees keeps the agent-first IA legible and low-friction Many co-equal dispatch surfaces + duplicated org views (read as cosmetic hierarchy, hunt-for-result)
D-027 2026-06-27 Claude Code ↔ K boundary K synthesizes a per-run config dir — three ownership domains (D1 host ~/.claude invoked-never-depended-on · D2 the K repo incl. versioned agent-config/ · D3 the per-run synthesized dir); synthesizeConfigDir relocates CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR per claude run (settings, per-tier --allowedTools, --mcp-config/--strict-mcp-config, vendored gitnexus skills/hook, injected L0 base operating prompt + L1 tier charter); auth token-first (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY/CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN) with a guarded host-credential fallback The agent engine is the host CLI, but a managed run must never inherit the host's skills/plugins/MCP/credentials — relocating the config dir per run makes the boundary product-clean and enforced (the host can't load), while injection keeps K's operating prompt + tier charter authoritative over any project CLAUDE.md Pointing runs at the host ~/.claude (leaks personal skills/plugins/MCP, non-reproducible); a static shared config dir (no per-tier allowlist/MCP scoping); prompt-only "ignore your host config" (unenforceable)
D-028 2026-06-27 artifacts/ + tasks/ are hidden, K-owned, never committed Gitignore the K-system dirs in every project — artifacts/ and tasks/ use the same hidden names everywhere (onboard.ts ensureGitignore); bibles scaffold to artifacts/bible/; K's own bible leaves version control and lives on-disk as a living spec These are K's working memory, not project source — committing them pollutes managed repos and couples K-internal state to project history; one consistent hidden location means any agent knows where to look without per-project config Versioning the bible/tasks into each repo (pollutes history, merge noise); a per-project config for the location (drift); deleting them (loses the living spec / tracker). Deferred to Phase 5: seeding + the work_items / agent_memory migration
D-029 2026-06-27 Host ~/.claude conservative cleanup Vendor what K needs, then offer a reversible host cleanup — gitnexus skills+hook are vendored into agent-config/; scripts/host-cleanup.mjs reconciles a dev host toward "invoked-never-depended-on" (dry-run default; --apply backs up first; removes only what K now owns) Once K vendors its dependencies the host copies are redundant, but a developer's host is personal — cleanup must be conservative and reversible, never surprising Auto-cleaning on boot (surprising, destructive); leaving everything (the host stays a hidden dependency); removing personal plugins too (superpowers/ecc/ui-ux are the operator's, left intact)
D-030 2026-06-27 Runtime Ollama model management (PLANNED 5.5) A persisted app_config store makes the active local model operator-selectable & hot-swappable — route() reads the active model + ENABLE_OLLAMA through getters (seeded from env), so a Settings selection applies to the next run with no restart; a model surface over the Ollama HTTP API (/api/tags list · /api/pull NDJSON progress on the EventBus→WS wire · /api/delete) plus a curated catalog and an fs.statfs disk-fit check; generation stays on the ollama run CLI The model was a boot-time env constant invisible to the operator — you couldn't see installed models, pull new ones, or switch without editing .env and restarting; a tiny config store + the Ollama HTTP API make it a live, observable surface while leaving the generation path and the ModelRouter contract untouched (the same grow-into-it posture routing took) Env-var-only (restart to change, invisible); shelling ollama pull with no progress (opaque multi-GB downloads); free-form pull with no disk guard (fills the disk mid-download); a vector/registry service (over-built for a local model list)
D-031 2026-06-27 Voice transcription — push-to-talk (PLANNED 5.4) A new TranscriptionProvider B-seam + local Whisper, push-to-talk — transcribe(audio)→{text} with a default impl POSTing to a local OpenAI-compatible Whisper server (faster-whisper, WHISPER_BASE_URL); browser MediaRecorder captures hold-to-talk audio → POST /api/transcribe (core proxies, browser holds no key) → transcript drops into the K composer / HITL reply as ordinary text; gated by ENABLE_VOICE, degrades to keyboard when unreachable Talking to agents needs STT, and Ollama can't run speech models — so transcription is its own swappable seam, kept local-first so audio never leaves the box; proxying through core keeps any key server-side; reusing the existing composer/HITL input means nothing downstream changes; the seam lets a cloud STT swap in later without touching callers Browser Web Speech API (streams audio to Google/Microsoft, no Firefox, quality varies); cloud STT day one (API key + per-minute cost + audio off-box); routing STT through Ollama (no speech models); always-listening/wake-word (false-trigger risk, larger build — deferred)

The compat helpers are renamed projectTasksDb → projectWorkItemsDb — now the FIRST-CLASS project-scoped surface of the unified store (routes/projects.ts + syncIssues ride it with zero HTTP shape change; tasks-route characterization stays byte-identical). A partial UNIQUE index (project_id, issue_number) WHERE scope='project' AND issue_number IS NOT NULL (dedupe first: newest created_at, rowid-DESC tiebreak) closes the syncIssues double-mirror hole, and updateProjectTaskFromIssue binds project_id in its WHERE (cross-project defense). Bonus degrade: the GitHub poller skips projects whose localPath vanished (one warn per boot; additive Project.pathMissing) instead of erroring every 60s The d2a "frozen safety copy" turned out to be an active hazard, not a net: deletes silently un-happened at every boot and a resurrected issue row could duplicate against a re-synced one (the old index was non-unique); one final backfill + drop makes deletes durable permanently, and the unique index makes issue-mirroring idempotent by construction; keeping the helper layer (renamed, no longer a shim) preserves the proven route/sync contract with zero API churn Keeping the frozen copy + a tombstone table for deletes (a second store to drift — the copy WAS the bug); rerouting routes/sync onto new statements in the same wave (churns the characterization-pinned surface for no behavior gain); a full-table UNIQUE (NULL-issue and non-project rows must stay exempt — hence partial); leaving the poller ENOENT spam (a vanished path is a state, not a per-minute error)
D-059 2026-07-02 migrate() version gate (P5.7 B2) migrate() gains a PRAGMA user_version fast path: SCHEMA_VERSION (1 at introduction; bumped as migrations land — see D-061) is stamped only AFTER a successful full guarded-ALTER/backfill scan, and a connection whose DB already carries the stamp returns immediately — so the per-run stdio MCP children (up to 3 per K/Chief turn) skip the full scan. The bump-on-new-migration discipline is documented in db.ts at the constant: add a migration to migrateSlow() → bump SCHEMA_VERSION, and an older-stamped DB re-runs the scan and is re-stamped. Per-step idempotency (hasColumn/hasTable guards, duplicate-column tolerance, one-shot flags) stays as belt-and-suspenders for the slow path and for concurrent first-boots racing before the stamp lands The migration scan grew heavy (table rebuilds, backfills, window-function dedupes) and runs on EVERY connection open — the main boot AND each MCP child — so K/Chief turns paid the full scan repeatedly for a DB that was already current; the pragma lives in the DB file, so one successful migration stamps it for every later connection; stamping only on success means a failed migration retries on the next open instead of being masked Stamping before the scan completes (a failed migration would never retry); a version table instead of the pragma (an extra read + the pragma is the SQLite idiom); dropping the per-step guards once gated (the slow path still races on concurrent first boots — belt-and-suspenders stays); leaving the scan ungated (per-child cost grows with every future migration)
D-060 2026-07-02 Lead-dispatch intents retire by LIVENESS DERIVATION, not a new status (P5.7 C2 conductor blocker) getActiveLeadDispatchByAssignment derives "active" via a LEFT JOIN on runs: 'pending' is always active; 'dispatched' is active only while genuinely in flight — the claim window (lead_run_id NULL, blocking fail-safe until the boot sweep) OR its lead run still non-terminal. A completed intent is retired-by-derivation with no status write. dispatch_lead's guard 1 is liveness-derived too (blocks only a LIVE linked run via isTerminalRunStatus), which unwedges Chief re-dispatch of completed assignments AND the new operator reassign — PATCH /api/chief/assignments/:id (IMMEDIATE transaction; 409 while the lead run is live or a dispatch intent is in flight, 400 same-lead; clears the stale lead_run_id; files a durable mgmt audit report) lead_dispatches has NO success-terminal status (CHECK pending|dispatched|failed) and the relay deliberately leaves a completed intent 'dispatched' forever — but the old statement counted every 'dispatched' row as active, so ONE successful dispatch wedged its assignment permanently (reassign 409'd forever; a follow-up dispatch_lead wave was impossible). Deriving liveness from the linked run matches the org-tree philosophy — the runs table is already the terminal-status truth, so the queue never needs a writer to keep a success status in sync A 'completed' status on the queue (rejected — SQLite CHECK change needs a table rebuild, AND it needs a run-terminal subscriber writing it forever, which can drift from the runs truth the way this bug did); clearing lead_run_id on terminal (destroys the durable parent→child link the org tree derives from); loosening the run-less-orphan case (it must keep blocking fail-safe — only the boot sweep can prove the run never spawned)
D-061 2026-07-02 Hard tool ceiling at the CLI + as-built chief charter + seed-row resync (P5.7 L-fix; from the failed live chain proof) The tier tool ceiling is now ENFORCED at the CLI via --disallowedTools: CAPABILITY_TOOL_UNIVERSE (Bash · PowerShell · Write · Edit · NotebookEdit · Task · Agent · WebFetch · WebSearch) is the authority-bearing built-in set; every managed run's denylist = the universe minus its granted tools (authority.ts::computeDisallowedTools; Task/Agent treated as ONE spawn capability under two CLI names), emitted after --allowedTools (empty ⇒ no flag, mirroring the dangling-flag guard). PowerShell + NotebookEdit join the orchestrator allowlist so leads lose nothing. chief.charter.md rewritten from its "PLANNED" stub to the operating procedure (assignment_list/report_list on wake; assign_lead → scope_projects → pick_workflow → dispatch_lead records an INTENT the main process executes; report; honesty rule: missing/failing tool → report + stop, never engineer) with content-pin tests on the procedure order + clauses. One-shot mig_seed_profile_authority_resync overwrites the 8 durable seed rows' authority arrays from resolveAuthority(charter) (operator-created profiles untouched; default_model untouched) — and forces the FIRST real SCHEMA_VERSION bump (1→2) under D-059's discipline The live K→Chief→lead→PR proof failed at the Chief hop: --allowedTools is only an AUTO-APPROVAL list and --permission-mode acceptEdits auto-approved Write/Edit for the no-code chief tier, so the Chief — whose stale "PLANNED" charter gave it no tool procedure — improvised: spawned an inline Agent subagent and WROTE FILES in its worktree, with only headless permission denial stopping the git/PR steps. Separately, upgraded DBs still carried pre-B1 seed rows (the real DB's chief lacked mcp__mgmt), so B1's row enforcement would have stripped the Chief's management server on exactly the DBs that upgraded Prompt-only discipline (the incident IS the counter-example — D-021 again); switching tiers to permission-mode default (breaks every autonomous run for one tier's ceiling); denying the whole tool namespace (Skill/TodoWrite/etc. are conveniences, not capabilities — denying them regresses leads for zero authority gain); a recurring resync (would clobber operator customizations now that the editors are real — hence one-shot); leaving stale seed rows to hand-editing (the upgrade path must be code, not operator surgery)
D-062 2026-07-04 K secretary lifecycle — resumable one-shot runs (supersedes the D-023/D-042 warm/fresh hybrid FOR K) A K ask is a RESUMABLE ONE-SHOT run, not a parked interactive process. Each K thread owns a stable Claude CLI session — --session-id <uuid> on the FIRST ask (seeded with the full renderSeed transcript), --resume <id> on every LATER ask sending only the new message — plus a stable per-thread config dir + cwd under <dataDir>/k-secretary/<threadId>/ (no worktree — K writes no code). A cold ask no longer re-pays the ~24k system-prompt + MCP-tool-schema envelope or replays the 12-turn transcript; continuity is a cheap cache-read of the resumed CLI session, while the durable k_threads/k_thread_turns stays the source of truth. The run ANSWERS AND EXITS (done) — it never parks at awaiting_input holding a live process + worktree (fixes F-054 cost/park; subsumes H10 self-terminate). cli_session_id persists ONLY on the first ask's successful done (SCHEMA_VERSION 5→6); undo (undoK / POST /api/k/undo) removes the dangling user turn so an undone ask is never replayed (guarded against a late kill-flush resurrecting it). GATED to k-secretary: regular dispatch runs are byte-for-byte unchanged (fresh worktree, ephemeral config, interactive HITL park). askK's warm flag is kept for API compatibility but is now always false; the awaiting_input branch remains only as belt-and-suspenders for any interactive reuse The D-023/D-042 hybrid kept a warm interactive process while chatting and re-seeded a fresh run when cold — but a parked K run held a live CLI process + worktree indefinitely (the F-054 cost/leak, the H10 self-terminate), and each cold re-seed re-paid the whole prompt/tool envelope AND replayed the transcript (a cost explosion on every ask). A resumable one-shot gets continuity AND boundedness for free: the CLI's own resumed session carries context cheaply, the durable thread survives reload/reset, and the run exits instead of squatting a process. Only K's lifetime changed — regular dispatch keeps interactive parking Holding a warm interactive K process (the D-023/D-042 model — the leak/cost bug this fixes); re-seeding a fresh run per cold ask (re-pays the envelope + replays the transcript — the cost explosion); giving K a worktree (it writes no code); dropping the durable thread in favor of the CLI session (the session is a cache, not the truth — reload/reset must recover from the thread); parking at awaiting_input (a one-shot has a single terminal boundary — the branch stays only for interactive reuse)
D-063 2026-07-04 Org lead roster + fail-fast lead naming — unblock the autonomous K→Chief→lead chain Give the Chief the authoritative lead roster and make lead-naming fail-fast at ASSIGN time. A new read-only lead_list mgmt tool returns the dispatchable leads (id · name · discipline · one-line role); the AUTO paths — the Chief's wake-goal seed, its no-hint delegation seed, and its charter — INJECT the five lead identifiers so the Chief always references a real lead and never invents a discipline like "engineering". assign_lead now VALIDATES the lead via resolveLeadProfileId — the SAME resolver dispatch_lead uses (by id, then name, then discipline slug, gated by isLeadProfile) — and REJECTS an unknown lead at assign time, so the two can never disagree. A project-scoped lead dispatch now BINDS a workflow_runs row + seeds its steps from the delegation workflow, so the lead's kstore status-write tools (workflow_step_set/workflow_status_set) RESOLVE against a real checklist; checklist seeding is isolated so a failure degrades to no-checklist without dropping the Chief/K report-back, and finalizeWorkflowRun reconciles lingering steps to blocked so the checklist can't contradict a completed run. (F-067/F-070/F-072/F-081) The live K→Chief→lead chain died at the lead-naming hop: the Chief, with no roster in its seed, invented a discipline that assign_lead ACCEPTED (storage) but dispatch_lead later REJECTED, so the objective stranded as a dangling assignment. Handing the Chief the authoritative roster + making assign_lead fail the same way dispatch_lead does makes the accept and the dispatch agree; binding a workflow_runs checklist gives the dispatched lead a real progress surface the status-write tools resolve against instead of improvising Leaving assign_lead permissive (the accepted-then-dangling assignment — the exact stall this fixes); a second, divergent resolver for assign vs. dispatch (they MUST agree — one resolveLeadProfileId); the lead improvising with no workflow_runs row (the status-write tools no-op with nothing to resolve); failing the whole dispatch when checklist seeding throws (degrade to no-checklist, keep the report-back)
D-064 2026-07-04 Health score PRORATES over measured dimensions — no phantom 100 computeHealthScore prorates over only the dimensions it could actually measure: score = round( Σ earned(measured) / Σ weight(measured) · 100 ). An UNMEASURED dimension is excluded from BOTH numerator and denominator — it neither helps nor hurts, never scored 0 nor full-credit. If NOTHING is measured the score is null (insufficient signal), NOT 100 — a freshly-onboarded project no longer reads 100/100 off scaffold files that never ran. The bible dimension is scored on AUTHORED content (the BIBLE_SCAFFOLD_MARKER sentinel: a bare scaffold is unmeasured; an authored bible — even uncommitted, per D-028 — is full marks), NOT git commit history, so an authored-but-uncommitted bible isn't punished. A MEASURED problem still demerits its own dimension exactly as before. verification_reports.score + score_breakdown become NULLABLE (a table rebuild, SCHEMA_VERSION 4→5), null-safe across the API and every web consumer. (F-032) The old fixed-weight 40·CI + 20·coverage + 20·bible + 20·findings formula credited a bare project for dimensions that carried no real signal (an unmeasured factor scored as neutral full marks), so a scaffold-only project read ~100/100 — a health lie that made a never-run project look healthier than a real one with findings. Prorating over measured dimensions makes the score reflect only real evidence; a zero-signal project is honestly null (insufficient signal) rather than a fabricated 100 Scoring an unmeasured dimension 0 (punishes a project for not emitting a signal — e.g. no coverage instrumentation); scoring it neutral/full (the phantom-100 bug); keeping score NOT NULL (can't represent "insufficient signal" — hence the nullable rebuild); scoring the bible on commit freshness (punishes an authored-but-uncommitted bible, which D-028 makes the norm for K's own gitignored bible)
D-065 2026-07-04 A bible is edited per-SECTION with source-of-truth writeback (refines D-002) A project/harness bible is edited per SECTION, and Save writes back to the section SOURCE. PUT /api/artifacts/:slug/sections/:sectionSlug (saveBibleSection) writes the edited body back to its canonical <bibleDir>/sections/<slug>.md source (frontmatter preserved via a lossless splitFrontmatter) THEN recompiles — so the edit IS the source and survives every recompile. The old whole-markdown PUT against a bible slug is REJECTED (400, steering the caller to the section editor), closing the silent-data-loss path (F-029, HIGH). Path-traversal-safe (SLUG_RE + manifest allowlist + isPathWithin). A project bible save no longer writes project-<id>-bible.* into K's OWN ARTIFACTS_DIR or clobbers the row's html_path — everything stays in the project's own dir (F-030); a project's Artifacts tab lists only its own project-<id>-* artifacts, not the harness globals (F-038) Under D-002 a bible compiles from section sources to one HTML; but editing and saving the COMBINED markdown (or the compiled HTML) was silently discarded on the next recompile — a real data-loss path. Writing the edit back to the exact section source the compiler reads makes the round-trip lossless; scoping the write to the project's own bible dir stops a project edit from leaking into K's artifacts and clobbering the row's html_path Editing/saving the combined bible markdown or compiled HTML (lost on recompile — the data-loss bug); writing project bibles into K's ARTIFACTS_DIR (cross-repo leak + html_path clobber); a free-form section path (traversal — hence SLUG_RE + manifest allowlist + isPathWithin)
D-066 2026-07-04 Terminal env scrub + prod-token exclusion + surfaced/opt-out credential posture Three defense-in-depth hardenings around the browser terminal + host credentials. (a) The terminal's node-pty no longer inherits process.env — it runs a SCRUBBED env (denylist: vendor prefixes + *_TOKEN/*_KEY/*_SECRET/*_PASSWORD + connection-string *_URL/*_URI/*_DSN/*_PASS/*_PWD/CONNECTION; PATH/HOME/PWD/etc. survive), so a leaked/guessed terminal token can't echo $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY. (b) VITE_TERMINAL_TOKEN and VITE_HARNESS_TOKEN are emitted undefined in a PROD bundle (the dev literal only under vite serve) and TerminalPage's fallback is dev-gated — prod fails CLOSED instead of shipping dev-terminal-token/dev-token-change-me. (c) A non-loopback + ENABLE_TERMINAL bind now REFUSES to boot without an explicit TERMINAL_ALLOW_REMOTE=true, even with a strong TERMINAL_TOKEN (was only refusing weak tokens). Plus the credential posture (managed | host-fallback | disabled) is SURFACED on /api/status + Settings + a boot summary (never the secret), so silent host-credential use is visible, and K_DISABLE_HOST_CREDENTIAL_FALLBACK opts a deployment OUT (fail-closed to unauthenticated). DEFAULT (no flag) is byte-identical — OOTB dogfooding unbroken. (F-084/F-064/F-090) The terminal ran with the full process env, so a guessed/leaked terminal token was a full credential-exfil channel; a prod build baked the dev terminal/harness tokens into client JS; a non-loopback terminal bind exposed a shell on the LAN behind only a token; and the D-027 host-credential dogfooding fallback copied host creds SILENTLY. Each is closed without moving the OOTB default — the scrub is additive, the prod-token exclusion only affects release builds, the remote-terminal gate is opt-in, and the posture is observable + opt-out-able Trusting the terminal token alone (one leak = full env exfil — hence the scrub); shipping the dev token literal in prod (fail closed instead); allowing a non-loopback terminal on a strong token alone (an accidental LAN bind exposes a shell — require TERMINAL_ALLOW_REMOTE); turning the host-fallback OFF by default (breaks OOTB dogfooding — make it VISIBLE + opt-out instead)
D-067 2026-07-04 Opt-in carry-working-tree dispatch mode An opt-in carryWorkingTree flag lets a dispatched run see the source repo's uncommitted work. Threaded POST /api/runs → startRun → startAgentRun (boolean-validated); when set, the run's worktree is seeded with the SOURCE repo's uncommitted tracked + staged changes via a non-destructive git stash create + apply (untracked NOT carried — documented; stash push -u avoided as it mutates the source). The DEFAULT (no flag) is BYTE-IDENTICAL — worktree add --detach HEAD unchanged, carry unreachable without the flag — locked by real-git tests incl. the persistent-session no-op interaction A dispatched run always started from a clean HEAD, so it could not see in-progress local edits — sometimes exactly the state you want an agent to start from. An opt-in carry lets a run observe the uncommitted tracked+staged tree without touching the default clean-worktree contract or mutating the source repo Carrying by default (changes the clean-worktree contract every run relies on); git stash push -u (mutates the source working tree — used the non-destructive stash create/apply); carrying untracked files (broader, unpredictable blast radius — tracked+staged only, documented)
D-068 2026-07-04 Post-final-review follow-up hardenings — org-model warn, killed-run cost honesty, /end relay-run termination, undo CLI-session scrub, skill-source realpath Five hardenings landed after the whole-implementation FINAL REVIEW (3 read-only agents — correctness / security / tests+docs — verdict STANDARDS HELD · SECURE · SOUND), each its own reviewable commit with a real regression test. (a) WARN-ONLY org model capability: isOrgToolCapableModel / warnIfOrgRoleModelNotToolCapable emit ONE clear warning at the Chief (wakeChief) and lead (drainLeadDispatches) dispatch choke-points when the role resolves to a haiku-tier model (which can't reliably drive the deferred MCP management tools, silently stalling the K→Chief→lead chain) — but still dispatch EXACTLY as configured (no upgrade, no refuse). (b) KILLED-RUN COST HONESTY: a run killed mid-turn never emits the authoritative turn-end result usage, so its 0s are UNMEASURED, not free — accumulateInterimUsage recovers per-assistant interim tokens (OUTPUT summed across messages, INPUT the conservative MAX so recovery can only under-count, never over-bill) via reconcileKilledUsage ONLY when no result was ever summed (never double-counts a completed turn); cost stays honestly unrecoverable (no interim cost signal); isUnmeasuredKilledRun keeps a genuinely-unmeasured killed run (0/0/$0) COUNTED as a run but OUT of the measured cost/token aggregates so it never reads as a $0 that drags an average. (c) /end flips a relay-dispatched LEAD run (was a no-op for non-interactive runs) by routing a non-interactive live proc through the same kill() path /kill uses → honest killed terminal; the interactive graceful-EOF path stays byte-identical (change scoped to !interactive). (d) undoK nulls the owning thread's cli_session_id (keyed on thread PK, resolved BEFORE the turn-delete) so an undone RESUME ask can't survive in K's live claude --resume session — closes the F-054/F-060 taint on the common fast-one-shot-then-undo path (active_run_id is already NULL by then). (e) readSkillSource realpath gate: a fail-safe fs.realpathSync check so a symlink planted inside agent-config/skills can't read THROUGH the F-069 confinement. The final review confirmed the 92-finding campaign did NOT diminish standards, but surfaced these five: one MEDIUM undo-session gap (undo removed durable turns but not the resumable CLI session), the three live-smoke follow-ups (haiku-Chief stall, killed-run $0, /end relay no-op), and a LOW skill-confinement symlink gap. Each strengthens an existing HIGH redesign or closes an honest accounting/termination gap WITHOUT changing default behavior (a) auto-upgrade to sonnet, or refuse the dispatch — both override operator intent; chose warn-only. (b) make Run cost/token fields nullable (shared-type ripple — kept honesty inside supervisor+metrics) or fabricate a cost estimate (rejected — no interim cost signal, would over-bill). (c) invent a distinct ended status (no graceful path exists for a non-PTY proc — killed is honest). (d) a new cli_session_run_id stamp column + keyed clear (schema 6→7 — thread-PK clear is simpler and errs safe by re-seeding). (e) also case-normalize isPathWithin on Windows (shared primitive, already fails safe — left a tracked follow-up rather than destabilize it pre-merge)

D-070 2026-07-05 Discovered-MCP trust gating — default-disabled, hash pinning, synth re-verify A discovered host MCP server is code execution by design, so it is gated twice: operator TRUST, then ENABLE.
