Limits: 25 iterations · 10-min run · 60s MCP tool calls · a repeated-identical-call detector · a >85% context-pressure warning "Skills regardless of model" needs a tool loop the CLI provides only for claude; owning the loop in-process gives local runs the same assets, event shapes, and observability with zero UI change, while degrade-in-place + the truthful badge preserve the §02 routing-honesty contract (choosing ollama can never silently run claude) Keeping the ollama run prompt pipe as the only engine (no tools, no MCP — parity impossible); silently falling back to claude for tool-less models (violates routing honesty); a child-process loop runner (the supervisor already owns lifecycle — in-process + AbortController is simpler and kill-safe); content-JSON tool-call salvage (strict structured tool_calls v1 — salvage is a documented non-goal)
D-073 (DRAFT — P0 probe verdict; finalize in P6/E-21) 2026-07-07 E-21 mid-run steering mechanism Design X — interrupt steering (pick iff scenario A showed control_response and/or an early result AND the post-interrupt turn answered): the supervisor keeps stream-json stdin open for ALL managed claude runs and exposes interrupt(runId) + steer(runId, text) — a steer during a turn = control_request interrupt then inject the operator turn. Evidence: core/probes/out/steering-{a,b}.jsonl — A: control_response=yes result_after_interrupt=yes post_interrupt_turn_answered=yes (interrupt ack subtype success; the aborted turn ends with an early error_during_execution result; the injected turn is answered STEER-ACK on the same stdin); B: results=2 queued_turn_answered=yes stream_survived=yes exit=0; measured $0.0423 Probe-measured on the real CLI (2026-07-07, haiku) — the stdin wire behavior is a fact, not a guess; committing to the strongest mechanism the CLI actually supports keeps E-21 buildable in P6 without re-probing Design Y — queued-turn steering (its pick-criterion required scenario A to fail, but A succeeded on all three counts; B's queued_turn_answered=yes stays recorded as evidence that a non-abort soft steer also works under Design X); Design Z — park-and-steer fallback (disqualified: both stronger mechanisms work on the wire); rewriting dispatch onto the Agent SDK (out of scope — the CLI wrapper is the product)
D-074 (DRAFT — P0 probe verdict; finalize in P6/E-22) 2026-07-07 E-22 follow-up-run mechanism Resume-based follow-ups (pick iff same-cwd resume retained context): a follow-up run spawns claude -p --resume <runs.cli_session_id> (column landed in P0, v8); if different-cwd resume ALSO retained context (NO), follow-ups run in a fresh worktree, else E-22 must retain the original run cwd/worktree until follow-ups close. EITHER WAY managed runs synthesize a per-run CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR that today is CLEANED at terminal (agent-config.ts synth.cleanup()) — E-22 must retain the config dir for resumable runs, mirroring the k-secretary persistentSession persist:true pattern. Evidence: core/probes/out/resume-*.jsonl — same-cwd retained: YES (answered "PLUM-42" verbatim); diff-cwd retained: NO (exit 1, stderr "No conversation found with session ID" — CLI session storage is keyed by project path); measured $0.0301 Probe-measured on the real CLI; --resume (a cheap cache-read continuation, the proven F-054 K-thread mechanism) is strictly better than replaying transcripts when it works — the probe tells us if it does Transcript-reseed fallback (disqualified: same-cwd --resume retained the planted secret verbatim, so replaying stored events is unnecessary); holding warm processes per run (the rejected pre-F-054 pattern — resource-heavy, crash-fragile)
D-075 2026-07-08 Run diffs derive from the k-checkpoint chain A run's reviewable diff is computed from its k-checkpoint chain — base = the first checkpoint's parent (the HEAD the run started from), head = the chain tip — durable and DETERMINISTIC: the refs live in the shared .git of the run's source repo and survive worktree removal, so a finished run's diff is always recomputable; mid-run the diff lags the live tree by at most ONE wave (the take-latest scheduler). PR diffs come from gh pr diff. BOTH producers normalize through ONE parser (diff-parse.ts::parseUnifiedDiff, pure, byte-capped at the last complete file boundary) into ONE DiffPayload, so the Review Deck renders one shape The run worktree is removed at terminal, so any diff computed from it dies with the run; the checkpoint chain is the only durable, replayable record of what the run changed — and one parser/one payload means the checkpoint path and the PR path cannot drift apart in rendering Diffing the live worktree (gone at terminal; racy mid-run); separate parsers/shapes for checkpoint vs PR diffs (two renderers drift); persisting diff text in the DB (the chain already holds it in git — recompute is deterministic and cheaper than storing megabytes)
D-076 2026-07-08 E-04 verify — one current row per run, fresh checkpoint worktree, operator-authored recipes verify_results holds ONE current row per run (PK run_id, upsert — the chip reflects the latest battery, not a history). The E-04 battery executes in a FRESH temp worktree materialized from the run's FINAL checkpoint: it never blocks (or races) run cleanup and is re-runnable anytime. Recipes are operator-authored shell strings — CI-config trust level: the operator writes them, K executes them verbatim (bounded timeouts + kill-tree). No recipe → NO row (the chip stays absent — never a fabricated pass) The run worktree is already gone when a terminal done emits, and the final checkpoint is guaranteed by the terminal snapshot — a fresh tree from it is always available, isolated, and disposable; operator-authored recipes carry exactly the trust an operator already places in their own CI config, so K adds no new execution authority A verify-history table (the chip is a current-state signal; history = re-run); verifying inside the run worktree (races terminal cleanup); auto-detected or agent-authored recipes (recipes are arbitrary shell — authorship must stay with the operator); blocking terminal status on the battery (async, never in the run's critical path)
D-077 2026-07-08 Rewind = a NEW run via baseCommit; the original run is immutable Rewind dispatches a NEW run whose worktree is created AT the chosen checkpoint via StartRunOptions.baseCommit — the original run, its chain, and its events are never mutated. Review fix runs (request-changes) use the SAME seam with baseCommit = the reviewed run's final checkpoint; baseCommit and carryWorkingTree are mutually exclusive by explicit guard (both dictate the worktree's starting tree). Conversation rewind is OUT of scope — this is code-state rewind only. Retention: a boot sweep deletes refs/k-checkpoints/* refs whose run rows are gone, under a documented SINGLE-CORE-PER-REPO assumption (ref existence is checked against this instance's DB only; cross-instance guard deferred to P7 fleet work) Immutability is the trust property — a review surface you can rewind FROM must never be rewritten BY the rewind; reusing the one dispatch seam means rewind and fix runs inherit every existing lifecycle guarantee (checkpointing, events, cleanup) for free Resetting the original run's worktree/chain in place (destroys the reviewed record); conversation rewind (a transcript concern, not a code-state one — deferred); eager ref deletion at run terminal (the review surfaces read finished runs — refs must outlive them); a cross-instance ref guard now (the instance lock is per-data-dir; deferred to P7 rather than half-built)
D-078 2026-07-08 Verify/impact GitNexus leg reads the exported graph OFFLINE E-04's change-scope and E-07's impact panel map changed files to indexed symbols by reading the project's exported .gitnexus/graph.json (written by graph.ts::exportGraphJson) — deliberately NO MCP and NO CLI in the hot path: the installed gitnexus CLI has no detect-changes subcommand, and the exported artifact is the already-built source of truth. Structural risk thresholds live in riskForScope (gitnexus-scope.ts); unindexed projects degrade to indexed:false, a missing/garbled artifact to null — never a blocked verify or panel The verify battery and the impact panel are post-run/read paths that must stay fast and non-blocking; a file read of an existing artifact is orders cheaper and failure-isolated compared to spawning a server or shelling out per call The gitnexus CLI in the hot path (no detect-changes subcommand exists); an MCP round-trip per verify/impact call (spawns a server process — slow, blocking, failure-coupled); re-analyzing the repo at verify time (heavy; the exported graph is the indexed truth until the next analyze)
D-079 2026-07-09 Plan gate — the awaiting_plan process-dead park (Phase 2) awaiting_plan is a DISTINCT process-dead park (canonical waiting/review_needed); resume = --resume runs.cli_session_id in the SAME worktree with the SAME preserved per-run config dir (D-074 composes); boot sweep flips only broken substrates to interrupted. A plan gate must survive a reboot and cost nothing while it waits — which a live-stdin awaiting_input park (D-014) cannot — so the process EXITS and the plan turn is replayed cheaply via the proven --resume continuation (D-062/D-074) in the same cwd + preserved config dir; a healthy parked run must never be swept to interrupted on boot Holding a live process at the plan gate (the D-014 cost/park, not reboot-survivable); resuming in a fresh worktree or config dir (D-074 proved diff-cwd resume loses the CLI session); a blanket boot sweep that interrupts every parked run (would destroy reboot-survivable plan parks — only BROKEN substrates flip)
D-080 2026-07-09 Plan storage — run_plans, one current row per run Plan storage = run_plans, ONE current row per run; last-wins operator edits (edited flag); raw always kept; unparseable plan turns park honestly with plan = NULL. The plan chip reflects the CURRENT plan the operator is approving, not a history — so one upsert row per run mirrors the verify_results current-row shape (D-076); keeping raw and honoring last-wins operator edits means what is approved is exactly what is shown, and a plan turn the CLI did not emit parseably must never fabricate a plan A plan-history table (the gate reads current state, not a log — matches D-076); discarding raw after parsing (loses the honest record when parsing degrades); dropping or blocking a run whose plan turn is unparseable (parking with plan = NULL keeps the operator in control instead)
D-081 2026-07-09 Approvals Inbox — a union query, never a table The Approvals Inbox is a UNION QUERY over five sources, never a table; dismissals: runs.reviewed_at stamp-once (backfilled at v10) and MCP inbox_dismissed_hash pinned to config_hash (drift re-surfaces). Every needs-YOU item already lives in its own authoritative source (plan approvals, awaiting_input parks, pending lessons, untrusted MCP, review-ready runs), so a derived union stays truthful with zero sync; a stamp-once reviewed_at and a config_hash-pinned MCP dismissal keep a dismissed card gone only until the underlying config actually changes A materialized inbox table (duplicates the five sources, drifts, needs sync); a permanent MCP dismissal not keyed to config_hash (a later config change would stay silently hidden — pinning re-surfaces the card)
D-082 2026-07-09 Notifications phase 1 — seeded rules + durable center + transient browser leg Notifications phase 1 = seeded notification_rules (event key -> in-app/browser) + a durable in-app center + a transient WS notification member carrying the rule's browser flag; engine dedupes on STATUS TRANSITIONS; the browser leg raises only when hidden + permission granted (gesture-requested). Operators need a durable record plus an optional attention-grab, but a browser toast on every event is noise — so a seeded rules table maps event key to channel, the in-app center is the durable log, and the browser leg fires only on a real status transition while the tab is hidden and permission has been granted Firing a browser notification on every event (noise — dedupe on transitions instead); a browser-only scheme with no durable center (misses everything while the tab is closed); requesting notification permission unprompted (browsers reject un-gestured prompts — request on an operator gesture)
D-083 2026-07-09 Verification is GitHub-native — the k/verify commit status Verification is GitHub-native: context k/verify published onto the run's final checkpoint sha; running->pending . pass->success . fail->failure . error->error . skipped->NOT published; publish at approve (with a fresh-PR cache upsert) + republish on verify updates for PR-linked runs (headRefName === k-review/<runId8>); merge = gh pr merge --merge guarded by an OPEN + checks-green readback; auto-merge defaults OFF and never bypasses the readback. The delivery path already lives on GitHub (D-003/D-004), so publishing verify as a native commit status makes it visible on the PR, in branch protection, and to any other tool with no new plumbing; a skipped recipe publishes nothing (never a fabricated pass — D-076), and a merge proceeds only after a fresh server-side OPEN + green readback so a stale UI can never merge a red PR A K-private verify signal invisible to GitHub branch protection (D-004 is GitHub-native gating); publishing a status for a skipped recipe (fabricates a pass — D-076 forbids it); merging on cached UI state without a readback (races a red rollup); auto-merge on by default (removes the human gate)
D-084 2026-07-09 Plan-gate tier default rides agent_profiles.plan_gate Plan-gate tier default rides agent_profiles.plan_gate (the org-default orchestrator row is the operator default; org dispatches inherit their profile's flag); an explicit per-dispatch planGate always wins. Whether a run stops at a plan gate is a policy that belongs on the same profile row that already carries a tier's tools/MCP/authority (D-021/D-047), so the org-default orchestrator row is the one operator-editable default and org dispatches inherit their profile's flag — while a specific dispatch can always force the gate on or off A single global on/off flag (cannot vary the gate per tier/lead); the gate always on or always off (removes operator control over routine vs. risky work); a per-dispatch flag a profile default could override (an explicit request must win)
D-085 2026-07-10 Org Timeline feed is a read-time union projection, not a table E-09 Org Timeline is a read-time UNION PROJECTION over existing tables (run heads + review-ready notifications + verify_results + open-PR github_cache), never a persisted feed table; ONE ['feed'] query key (with a ['feed',500] timeline variant) powers K-home recent + the Timeline view; ActivityStrip is deliberately NOT re-pointed and stays the live-runs widget. A projection reuses the existing sources with no schema/write-path and cannot drift from them (mirrors the E-05 Inbox union); one shared key keeps the two historical surfaces consistent. ActivityStrip needs COMPLETE, uncapped coverage of every non-terminal run plus rich Run fields (prompt/agent) for its cards, which a capped lean historical feed cannot honestly provide, so forcing it onto the feed would silently drop parked runs under the item cap. A persisted org_events table (a schema bump + a write at every transition + a second source of truth that can drift); re-pointing ActivityStrip onto the capped feed (drops active/parked runs beyond the cap, a live-monitoring regression); three separate recent-activity queries (drift).
D-086 2026-07-10 Run Narrative = deterministic fields always + auto/degrade local-model bullets E-08 Run Narrative renders deterministic fields (goal/outcome/files/verification/cost) derived from a run's events on every open; <=3 Decisions / <=3 Risks bullets come from the LOCAL model, auto-attempted on open, labeled "generated", physically separated from the deterministic fields, and gracefully OMITTED (with a subtle note) when the model is unreachable/unparseable; a bounded 20s abort guarantees the endpoint never blocks. The card must be trustworthy and always useful: measured/derived facts can never be confused with model prose (labeling + separation), and an unavailable local model degrades to facts-only rather than blocking or erroring; the abort bounds a stuck /api/chat stream so the GET honours its own never-blocks contract. Mixing generated bullets into the deterministic fields (confuses fact and inference); requiring the local model (blocks the card when Ollama is down); an on-demand-only button (loses the auto glance); an unbounded model call (a stuck stream hangs the request).
D-087 2026-07-10 Cost lens is measured-only; token chips become relative weight bands E-13 rolls up MEASURED cost_usd (run->lead->project->day) and computes dispatch recent-actuals (median/p90 $/run, recency-windowed, cost_usd>0 pool, scope agent-profile->project->global fallback at n<5); catalog capability token chips become RELATIVE light/medium/heavy weight bands (derived from token counts, never a price); a committed no-price-tables grep gate enforces the no-price-coupling rule. The program bans price-coupled estimation (measured actuals only): recent-actuals are real historical costs, not price*tokens, and the agent->project->global ladder gives the most relevant number with a graceful thin-history fallback; weight bands convey relative context cost without implying a dollar figure; the grep gate makes the rule mechanically enforced. A price-per-token table or price*token estimate (confidently-wrong, banned); a single global actuals number (mixes cheap and expensive agents); dollar-denominated capability chips (implies billing the user does not incur).
D-088 2026-07-10 Single HealthRubric + compile-time glossary E-12 folds the ~5 drifting health-score->color/label copies into ONE healthRubric (canonical thresholds >=75 healthy / >=50 warn / else critical / null unknown) consumed by every web health surface; glossary terms are extracted from the bible glossary section at pnpm bible COMPILE time into a committed generated module feeding a GlossaryTerm tooltip, guarded by a core-side drift test. The server bible.ts liveHealth 80-threshold is intentionally left unchanged. One rubric means a threshold or palette change lands in a single place instead of drifting across ProjectCard/FleetGraph/ProjectVerification/ProjectWorkspace/verify; compile-time glossary extraction keeps tooltips in lockstep with the living spec at zero runtime cost with a drift guard; the server's distinct liveHealth threshold is a separate concern not swept in, to avoid changing server behavior. Per-site health color logic (drifts); a runtime glossary fetch (latency + another endpoint); silently changing the server 80 threshold to match web's 75 (an unrelated behavior change).
D-089 2026-07-10 Electron over Tauri for the desktop app Ship the desktop app as ELECTRON (electron-builder → NSIS + electron-updater); core is spawned as a Node-20 CHILD, not Electron's runtime. Core is all-Node with native modules (better-sqlite3 / node-pty) and spawns CLIs, so one JS/Node toolchain fits and electron-builder gives installer + updater for free; the ABI smoke (Electron ABI 130 ≠ core's ABI 115) is handled by spawning core under a bundled Node-20 — avoiding electron-rebuild and keeping core's ABI-115 test suite intact (see §15). Tauri (a Rust toolchain plus a standalone node.exe sidecar whose ABI match Tauri itself flagged make-or-break); running core IN Electron's runtime + electron-rebuild (breaks the shared pnpm store and core's ABI-115 natives/tests).
D-090 2026-07-10 Core serves the web SPA same-origin Core serves web/dist via @fastify/static (gated on K_WEB_DIST) so the packaged app is ONE origin — relative /api, same-host WS — with the bundle public and the data plane token-gated. One origin removes CORS and the build-frozen port entirely (the client's relative /api and window.location WS just work); the bundle already bakes no token, so making it public matches the existing Vite posture while every /api/*//ws* route stays bearer/query-token gated (only public GET/HEAD assets are auth-exempt). Tauri hosting web/dist and repointing /api + WS (needs runtime port injection into the bundle); a cross-port core + CORS (more surface, build-frozen port).
D-091 2026-07-10 Token handoff via IPC → auto-auth The shell reads core's on-disk auth token and seeds it into the renderer's sessionStorage['k.harnessToken'] via a SYNCHRONOUS IPC from a sandboxed preload, so the same-origin SPA auto-authenticates; the token never rides argv or the URL, and core's auth is unchanged. The SPA already reads sessionStorage['k.harnessToken'], so seeding it from a sandboxed preload before the first API call gives a no-login desktop experience with zero core change; keeping the token off argv/URL (IPC only) closes the two easiest leak paths, and best-effort seeding degrades to the normal login screen on any failure. Baking a token into the bundle (a prod build bakes none by design — it would be a shipped secret); an in-app login screen (needless friction for a local single-user app).
D-092 2026-07-10 Detect-&-guide host prerequisites A system doctor (GET /api/system/doctor + a Settings card) detects claude/git/node (required) and gh/ollama (optional); the non-redistributable claude CLI is guided-to-install, never bundled. The agent engine is the host claude CLI and it is not redistributable, so the honest move is to detect + guide (with install URLs) and report ok only when every required tool is present; probes never throw (a missing tool is data), so first-run readiness is a clear checklist instead of a mystery failure. Bundling the agent engine (not redistributable); assume-installed (a rough, opaque first run when a prerequisite is missing).
D-093 2026-07-10 Core relocatability via K_REPO_ROOT REPO_ROOT/ARTIFACTS_DIR/WORKSPACE_DIR honor a K_REPO_ROOT override, so a read-only install redirects writable repo-relative paths (.worktrees/artifacts/workspace) to a per-user writable runtime (seeded with the bible source on first run/version-change); agent-config stays read __dirname-relative from the read-only bundle. A packaged install is read-only but core must write worktrees/artifacts, so one env override moves exactly the write paths to <userData>/runtime while read-only assets (agent-config, including skills) stay bundled and __dirname-relative — the smallest seam that makes core relocatable without copying the whole repo. Shipping/running from a writable copy of the entire repo (heavy, and pollutes user data); leaving paths __dirname-relative (every write fails under a read-only install).
D-094 2026-07-10 Windows-first, unsigned, auto-update Ship Windows-only and UNSIGNED with electron-updater against public GitHub Releases; trust = HTTPS-to-GitHub + the sha512 in latest.yml (NO signature backstop → the release pipeline must be protected); SmartScreen warnings accepted. Proving the Windows install/tray/notification/update pattern end-to-end is the valuable first step, and an unsigned build still auto-updates (feed pinned at build time, sha512 pins the bytes) — provided the GitHub account/Actions pipeline that can publish a Release is locked down (2FA, branch protection, no direct-push releases), since that is the actual trust anchor. Code-signing now (a paid cert + notarization — deferred until the pattern is proven); no auto-update (manual re-download); cross-platform now (deferred until the Windows pattern is proven).
D-095 2026-07-11 Detect-&-guide is on-demand doctor + reactive first-run dialog Detect-&-guide (D-092) is SURFACED as (a) the always-available System Doctor card in Settings — the on-demand, re-checkable detect+guide surface — plus (b) a reactive native dialog when core fails to become healthy on launch (which points at the Node.js/PATH prerequisite). A proactive pre-flight probe and auto-opening the doctor on first run are deferred as a tracked UX enhancement, not shipped in this cut. Auto-surfacing on first run needs a first-run flag + web auto-navigation that is better designed as its own small change than rushed into the packaging cut — recorded here so the delivery is not an undocumented deviation from the Wave 2/3 plan wording. Blocking the plan on a literal pre-flight git --version/node --version + first-run auto-open (real UX value but a non-trivial web/first-run change late in the packaging effort); silently shipping the substitution with no decision record (the deviation would be invisible to a future reader).
D-096 2026-07-11 Rail flattened 14->9; legacy hashes soft-redirect (E-29, P4) P4 flattens the Direct/Observe rail into ONE 9-item flat rail (K · Org · Projects · Skills · Runs · Insights · Inbox + footer Help · Settings); merged/folded view strings are removed from KNOWN_VIEWS; a VIEW_REDIRECTS map + resolveRoute soft-redirect every legacy hash to its new home and useHashRoute canonicalizes the address bar via history.replaceState (no Back-trap). D-100 2026-07-11 E-11 — canonical run-status adoption completes (lib/status.ts) E-11 completes for canonical run-status surfaces: they adopt web/src/lib/status.ts (runStatusMeta / agentRunStatusMeta / delegationStatusMeta, all DEFAULT-LESS), and every adoption site GUARDS membership so an unknown status degrades to muted rather than throwing (skill_run rows use agent-run vocab -> agentRunStatusMeta, not runStatusMeta). D-102 2026-07-11 Message Dock replaces the ⌘K palette + Activity strip A single MessageDock component — a persistent composer BAR on Home, a floating circular FAB everywhere else — replaces both the ⌘K CommandBar palette and the live-only Activity strip as the one front door; the Ctrl/Cmd+K chord and every g+letter navigation chord are UNCHANGED. The palette (search+dispatch) and the strip (live-status ticker) were two separate UI languages for "talk to K" and "see what's running" that both duplicated the Home composer; one Dock with a shared <Composer/>, K's inline route preview, and the 5 s undo toast collapses them into one idiom — live-run visibility moves to the Runs badge + Insights, per metric-uniqueness. A dock-bar mounted on every route (a fixed bottom bar competes for vertical space on dense Runs/Insights surfaces); dropping the palette's fuzzy-navigate function (jump-to-anything is still needed; the Dock's thread-picker + K routing subsumes it).
D-103 2026-07-11 Multi-thread K = K conversation threads only k_threads/k_thread_turns model K's OWN conversational history — multiple named, renamable, archivable chat threads — and are DELIBERATELY NOT a second view onto agent run execution; a dispatched run's live/replay detail stays exclusively in the Runs console, cross-linked from a thread turn via a → view run chip carrying that turn's runId. Conflating "a chat with K" and "a run's event stream" into one thread model would force every run event through a chat-shaped (role+text) turn and lose the run console's structured tool-call/diff/timeline rendering; keeping the two data shapes separate and cross-linking by id lets each own what it actually needs. Modeling a run as appended K-thread turns (loses RunConsole's structured rendering); one unified polymorphic "activity" timeline (materially more complex for no operator-visible gain over a simple cross-link chip).
D-104 2026-07-11 Operator user-memories store + memory_save A user_memories table (UserMemory: content, optional sourceThreadId) holds durable operator facts/preferences, editable directly on Personal → Memories; K's memory_save MCP tool writes to the SAME store from inside a conversation with NO approval gate, but fires a quiet memory_saved in-app notification (never a modal) so a save is never silent. The tool and its "your current memories are…" prompt injection are wired into the SECRETARY (K) charter ONLY — not Chief, not the leads. A user memory is a fact ABOUT the operator, not an approved-lesson process change (§04's separately-gated lessons queue), so it needs no review gate — but a background write with zero signal would still be surprising, hence the quiet notification. Scoping the tool and the injection to K's own charter keeps "knows the operator" a property of the one agent that actually talks to them. Gating memory_save behind the lesson accept/reject queue (wrong trust model — this is the operator's own data, not a proposed process change); injecting user memories into every agent's system prompt (unbounded prompt bloat for leads that never talk to the operator, and a scope leak of personal facts into engineering-only contexts).
D-105 2026-07-11 Widget grid, server-persisted Zod-validated layout Home's Overview tab is a fixed 3×3 grid of up to 9 catalog widgets (HomeWidgetIdSchema); placement (HomeLayoutSchema — in-bounds, non-overlapping, no duplicate ids, .strict()) persists server-side via GET/PUT /api/settings/home-layout (Zod safeParse, 400 on an invalid layout) through the existing config-store.ts, with an optimistic client-side write (useHomeLayout) so customize-mode feels instant. Session/localStorage-only placement resets silently on a new device or a cleared browser; an unvalidated layout risks an out-of-bounds or overlapping grid the renderer can't lay out. A server-side Zod schema makes an invalid layout impossible to persist, and reusing config-store keeps the storage layer boring rather than inventing a new one. A free-form (non-grid) drag-and-drop canvas (no bound on overlap/off-screen placement, no natural "empty cell" affordance for the add-picker); per-device-only local persistence (loses the layout on a new machine or browser profile).
D-106 2026-07-11 Legacy-hash redirect posture Every view string removed from KNOWN_VIEWS by this restructure keeps a VIEW_REDIRECTS entry mapping it (and its param/sub-param, where the destination can consume them) to its new home; useHashRoute applies the redirect via history.replaceState (never a push), so a bookmarked or shared legacy hash still lands correctly and Back never bounces through a dead intermediate hash. Silently 404-ing a link that worked yesterday breaks bookmarks, shared URLs, and muscle memory for no operator benefit; replaceState specifically (not a normal navigate) avoids a Back-button trap where Back from the canonical page would return to the legacy hash, which immediately re-redirects forward again. Routing every removed hash to the 404 page with a manual "did you mean" link (an unnecessary extra click for a redirect the router can resolve deterministically); a server-side redirect table (the app is a static SPA with hash routing — there is no server request to intercept).
D-107 2026-07-13 CHIEF_WAKE → a persisted Settings choice, default OFF (the single front door) — E-14..E-18 Turn the env CHIEF_WAKE flag into a persisted operator choice: a new Settings → "Autonomous Org" section (SettingsAutonomy.tsx) with a master enabled toggle (default OFF) + sub-toggles (Generate proposals / Auto-pull backlog / Self-heal failed runs) + max-concurrency + org daily budget cap + warn %, all one JSON blob in app_config (autonomy.settings, mirrors home_layout). Agents→Org shows only a read-only status chip deep-linking to it. Toggling ON at runtime needs NO restart — the schedulers always wire at boot and gate on the persisted setting per-tick. Autonomy is global org config with the same shape as every other operator-configurable system behavior (Ollama, Voice, Claude default model, Notifications, org-default authority), which already lives in Settings via config-store; making it a persisted default-OFF choice means a fresh DB is inert until the operator opts in, exactly as directed. Env-only flag defaulting ON (invisible, non-persistent, on by surprise); an Agents/Org control panel (duplicates the Settings pattern for one more surface); an always-on scheduler that reads the env each tick (still env-coupled, no operator UI).
D-108 2026-07-13 The master gates the whole stack; the budget governor is the always-on exception autonomySettings().enabled (default false) gates ALL FOUR autonomous behaviors — chief auto-wake, proposal collectors (&& proposals), backlog auto-pull (&& backlogAutoPull), self-heal (&& selfHeal); each sub-toggle additionally requires enabled. The budget governor is the ONE exception: a safety cap that applies to manual AND autonomous dispatches even while autonomy is OFF, once a cap is set. A single on/off front door the operator configures once is the honest model — a fresh DB with the master OFF does nothing autonomous. The budget cap is a safety limit, not an autonomous behavior, so gating it behind the master would let turning autonomy off silently disable the operator's own spend cap. Per-behavior independent enables with no master (no single front door — the exact fragmentation the directive rejected); gating the budget cap behind the master too (a safety cap that vanishes when autonomy is off is a footgun).
D-109 2026-07-13 Env CHIEF_WAKE deprecated warn-only chiefWakeEnabled() now reads the persisted autonomySettings().enabled; the env var no longer defaults autonomy ON and NO migration writes a persisted true from it. A one-time boot warning fires (regardless of enabled state) whenever CHIEF_WAKE is set in the environment, pointing the operator at Settings → Autonomous Org. Removing a shipped env flag silently would strand an operator who still sets it; warn-only keeps the boot honest while the persisted default (OFF) becomes the real gate. Refusing to migrate the env true into a persisted true avoids silently keeping autonomy on for anyone who had it enabled — the operator re-opts-in deliberately. A hard removal (breaks an existing .env with no notice); migrating CHIEF_WAKE=1 → persisted enabled:true (silently keeps autonomy on, defeating default-OFF); keeping the env as a co-equal gate (two sources of truth).
D-110 2026-07-13 E-16 routines reuse the Automations tab / skills table (no new table, no new rail slot) E-16 EXTENDS the existing AutomationsTab.tsx (Agents→Skills→Automations, already a node-cron registry over the skills table): an NL→cron helper that is RULES-ONLY (no model; an unmappable phrase → 400 at the route boundary), a next-run display, and MEASURED cost per routine (summed from its run history via a JOIN to runs.cost_usd). The Automations tab is already a full schedule registry; a routine is a schedule-triggered skill, so it needs only a derived next-run + measured cost, not a new entity — DRY over the master re-baseline's mapping, and no new destination in the 6-tab rail. A new routines table + rail slot (duplicates the skills scheduler and the no-new-rail rule); an LLM NL→cron translator (non-deterministic; a rules table suffices and fails closed on unmappable phrases).
D-111 2026-07-13 Proposals are blocked org work_items deduped by source_key (+ sticky-dismiss & one-shot-per-project honest limits) A proposal is an org-scoped work_item created status='blocked' with a source/source_key, deduped by a partial-unique source_key index, open-capped (20). Deterministic ZERO-TOKEN collectors (ci_failed / verify_finding / open_issue / stale_bible) run on a 15m cron. Personal→Inbox surfaces them as proposal cards: approve→open (enters the backlog); dismiss→cancelled (STICKY — the source_key still resolves to the cancelled row, so it never re-nags). Reuses the unified work_items store (D-026) with no new table; the sticky dismiss is the "don't nag again" signal. HONEST LIMITATION (deliberate, default-OFF): the project-keyed source_key (<source>:<projectId> for ci_failed/verify_finding/stale_bible; open_issue keys per issue) has no run/date discriminator, so such a proposal is effectively ONE-SHOT per project — once a row exists in ANY status (approved/done or dismissed) a genuine later recurrence is NOT re-surfaced until the row clears — and there is no undo on inbox dismiss. Documented as tracked follow-ups, not sold as continuous monitoring. A new proposals table (the work_items store already fits); a run/date-discriminated source_key (would re-nag on every 15m tick — a worse default); shipping an undo affordance now (deferred).
D-112 2026-07-13 Budget park = reactive refuse-with-reason, measured rolling-24h, zero forecasting (+ deferred operator-action routes) E-17 caps on MEASURED runs.cost_usd over a rolling 24h window — ZERO forecasting (no price×token, no projection). Org cap (autonomy settings) + per-project caps (projects.budget_daily_usd). At cap a dispatch is PARKED = refused-with-reason (429 / BudgetCapError), no queue — the operator raises the cap. Gated paths: startAgentRun (autonomous schedule/event/delegation), manual POST /api/runs (→429), operator→Chief delegateToChief (→429), and autonomous scheduled/event skill dispatch. EXEMPT: interactive/persistent K turns. Measured-only honors the program's no-price-coupling rule (the committed no-price-tables grep gate stays green); a reactive refuse-with-reason is simpler and truer than a queue or a projection; exempting interactive K keeps the operator's own conversation with their secretary unblocked. Operator-initiated action routes (rewind / review-fix / deep-verify / workflows / run-skill-now) are a tracked follow-up, documented honestly rather than claimed. Forecasting / "projected" spend (price-coupled, banned); a queue that runs the parked work later (hidden deferred spend); gating interactive K (blocks the operator talking to their own secretary).
D-113 2026-07-13 E-27 eval-derived lessons ride the D-041 lesson gate, off verify_results (eval_results deferred) Repeated (≥2) same-signature failures produce ONE deduped, capped (10) PENDING agent_memory lesson through the EXISTING D-041 lesson gate — reusing the lesson_pending inbox kind, no new UI. SCOPE: implemented off verify_results only; eval_results is DEFERRED (it has no free-text failure-reason column to signature on) as a documented extension point. This is memory layer C's first real slice; riding the existing human-gated lesson queue means a machine-derived lesson STILL needs operator approval (nothing self-modifies its brain), and no new surface is added. verify_results carries the free-text failure signature eval_results lacks, so it is the honest first source. A new auto-lesson table + UI (duplicates the D-041 gate); ungated auto-derived lessons (a bad lesson poisons the profile); claiming eval_results coverage now (it has no failure-reason column — would be a fabricated signature).
D-114 2026-07-13 UI Polish — liquid-glass design system: 4 tiers, gated icon set, 19-primitive kit, empty token gate A 26-task program re-skins the dashboard onto FOUR explicit glass tiers (glass-chrome/glass-panel/glass-overlay/surface-solid, picked by role with a hard no-nested-backdrop-filter rule and a reviewed ≤6-blurred-regions-per-viewport budget, replacing D-013/D-024's hero-only/dense-opaque binary); the full lucide-react icon set is closed behind ui/Icon's 33-entry ICONS map (the only file in web/src importing the library directly); a 14-file, 19-named-component primitive kit ships at web/src/ui/ (Button/IconButton, Dialog, EmptyState, ErrorState, Input/Textarea/Select, GlassPanel, Icon, KpiTile, SectionHeader, Skeleton/SkeletonRow/SkeletonTile, Spinner, StatusPill, Tag, Tooltip) alongside the pre-existing canonical Tabs/SegControl; and web/test/ui-token-gate.test.ts's allowlist — seeded at the program's start and shrunk wave by wave — reaches EMPTY ([], commit fcc3284), so every color in web/src now routes through a token or its Tailwind semantic class with zero exemptions. The prior binary (glass on hero surfaces, opaque on dense data) couldn't express nesting (a dialog floating over an already-glass chrome) or a numeric blur budget (an 11-tile KPI row blows the frame-rate budget even though KPI tiles read as a hero surface) — a graded, role-based tier system with an explicit nesting rule replaces per-component ad hoc calls with one discipline; gating icons behind one typed map keeps the icon vocabulary closed and auditable; a shrinking-to-empty allowlist makes "no raw color left in the app" a verifiable, continuously-enforced fact rather than a one-time claim. The --chart-1..8 dataviz palette was validated, not redesigned — a CVD check against the real dark surface caught one failing adjacent pair (chart-7↔chart-8, ΔE 1.1) fixed by snapping --chart-8 to #6366f1 (ΔE 15.4); a separate lightness-band check fails all 8 tokens against a generic heuristic and is accepted as non-actionable since the palette is brand-locked and contrast ≥3:1 still holds. A single opaque/glass switch per component (already broken by nesting cases the program had to resolve one-by-one); an automated blur-region-count lint (no static check reliably counts simultaneously-rendered, responsive-layout-dependent blur regions — kept as a manual per-wave and whole-app visual review instead); repainting the chart palette wholesale to satisfy the lightness-band heuristic (palette is brand-locked; the one real contrast failure needed only a single-token snap).
⌘

Operations

stable updated 2026-07-13

Running locally

# Prerequisites: Node 20, pnpm 10, claude CLI authenticated, gh CLI authenticated
pnpm install

pnpm --filter @k/core dev    # core API + WS  → http://localhost:3001
pnpm --filter @k/web dev     # dashboard      → http://localhost:5173
pnpm dev                     # both in parallel

Dev-stack hygiene

  • pnpm --filter @k/core dev is non-watch by default (F4) — one boot, no auto-restart on file save. Hot-restart-on-save is opt-in: pnpm --filter @k/core dev:watch.
  • Never leave a dev stack (watched or not) running across a git merge/rebase, or while hand-editing schema/migrations in core/src/db.ts — an in-place restart can boot mid-edit and stamp a schema version before its migration exists (see the poisoned-stamp incident under Schema migrations).
  • Before merging anything that touches core/src/db.ts, run pnpm smoke:upgrade (core/scripts/upgrade-smoke.mjs) — it copies the real dev DB to a temp dir, boots the merged core against the copy on port 3299, and polls /api/runs + /api/profiles, proving the upgrade path boots (not just a fresh install).

Environment (core/.env)

PORT=3001
HOST=127.0.0.1                        # loopback default; only set 0.0.0.0 behind Tailscale / an auth proxy (see Remote access)
HARNESS_TOKEN=                        # leave UNSET to auto-generate+persist a strong token on first run (see Remote access)
CORS_ORIGIN=http://localhost:5173
CLAUDE_MODEL=claude-sonnet-4-6        # first-run SEED for the runtime Claude default (now app_config-managed — change it in Settings, no restart)
RUN_PERMISSION_MODE=acceptEdits       # claude --permission-mode for worktree runs; default acceptEdits.
                                      #   one of default|plan|acceptEdits|bypassPermissions (invalid → acceptEdits + warn).
                                      #   only applied inside a disposable worktree; fallback-to-cwd runs stay default-restricted.
                                      #   bypassPermissions (allows Bash etc.) is explicit opt-in for fully-autonomous runs.
ENABLE_OLLAMA=false
GITHUB_POLL_MS=60000                  # gh polling interval
ENABLE_GITHUB_POLL=true               # set false to disable

Runtime config (no restart)

Several values above are now first-run seeds, not frozen constants. The Claude default model (CLAUDE_MODEL), the Ollama enable / base-url / active-model, and the voice settings are persisted in the app_config table and editable live from Settings — a change applies to the very next run without touching .env or restarting core. Each env var is consulted only when app_config has no stored value for its key (seed-then-override; config-store.ts). This retires the earlier recon finding that the Claude default model was an env-frozen const read once at router.ts load.

Data locations

What Where Versioned
SQLite (runs, events, artifacts, projects, verification) data/k.db (WAL) no
Harness bearer token (first-run generated secret) data/auth-token (0600, gitignored) no
Shipped agent-config assets (D2) agent-config/ (base prompt, tier charters, vendored gitnexus skills/hook) yes
Bible source artifacts/bible/ no — gitignored K-system dir (same in every project)
Compiled bible artifacts/project-bible.html no (generated)
Other artifacts artifacts/*.md (+ generated .html) no — gitignored K-system dir
Task tracker tasks/todo.md, tasks/lessons.md no — gitignored K-system dir
Per-run synthesized config dir (D3) <dataDir>/agent-runs/<runId>/config/ no — ephemeral, gitignored, swept on boot
Run worktrees .worktrees/<runId> git worktrees, pruned after run no — .worktrees/ gitignored (P5.7)
Cloned workspaces workspace/ no

artifacts/ and tasks/ are K-system directories — the same hidden, gitignored names in every managed project (onboard.ts ensureGitignore adds them); bibles scaffold into artifacts/bible/. K's own bible therefore lives on-disk as a living spec and is not under version control.

data/k.db is a dev database — disposable test/dev data only, never personal data; the only registered project is the K repo itself (jarvis-core → the repo root). Treat it as expendable: if it needs wiping to recover from a bad state, wipe it.

Bible workflow

  • Edit a section under artifacts/bible/sections/ (update its updated: frontmatter).
  • Recompile: restart core, or POST /api/bible/compile (bearer auth), or wait for the next startup.
  • The compiled HTML is self-contained — open it directly in a browser or via the dashboard Docs view.
  • Registered projects follow the same flow with <repo>/docs/bible/.
  • In-dashboard editing is per-SECTION (D-065). The Bible/Artifacts tab edits one section at a time; Save (PUT /api/artifacts/:slug/sections/:sectionSlug, saveBibleSection) writes the edited body back to that sections/<slug>.md source (frontmatter preserved) then recompiles — so the edit IS the source and survives every recompile. Editing the combined markdown of a bible slug is rejected (400 → the section editor), and a project-bible save stays in the project's own dir (never K's ARTIFACTS_DIR, never clobbering the row's html_path).

Onboard / verify scaffold-then-commit workflow

Onboarding (POST /api/projects/:id/onboard) and verification (POST /api/projects/:id/verify) may scaffold files into the working tree — uncommitted: a starter .github/workflows/ci.yml and/or a starter bible, written for operator review rather than pushed. Nothing is committed or pushed on your behalf. After running either, inspect the proposed changes with git status / git diff, then commit manually if you accept them. The score reflects the pre-fix state; the next verify observes the now-present files.

Host cleanup

Because K vendors what it needs (gitnexus skills/hook into agent-config/) and synthesizes a per-run config dir, the host ~/.claude is only ever invoked, never depended on. scripts/host-cleanup.mjs reconciles a developer's host install toward that posture: it is dry-run by default (prints what it would do), and --apply backs up first, then removes only what K now owns. It is deliberately conservative — personal plugins (superpowers / ecc / ui-ux) are left intact and the action is reversible.

Host capability catalog — rescan, trust, probe (D-069/D-070)

  • Every boot (first boot included), after seedBuiltinSkills, a guarded host scan (syncHostDiscovery) refreshes the capability catalog from the host layer — user / project / plugin skills + host MCP servers — and logs one summary line ([discovery] host capability scan: skills +a ~b -c, mcp +x ~y -z). The scan is try/catch-warned so a broken host install never bricks boot, and a malformed host artifact (unreadable dir, bad SKILL.md, non-stdio server) becomes a structured warning, never a throw. Everything discovered lands default-disabled.
  • Rescan on demand: POST /api/capabilities/rescan (the Skills-page Rescan button) → 200 + a RescanResult, and a capabilities_update WS broadcast. A rescan upserts by qualified key, never touches your enable overlay, flags vanished assets status='missing' (fail-closed at PATCH and synth), and auto-disables an MCP server whose host config drifted.
  • Discovery warnings surface in the rescan response warnings[] and the Skills-page banner — read them; a skipped source is invisible in the catalog until fixed.
  • Granting trust: before Trust & enable on a discovered MCP server, review the command, args, and env names the TrustDialog shows — trusting a server authorizes code execution inside every run that mounts it. Trust pins the config hash; a host-side config change auto-disables + clears trust (re-review to re-enable). Env values never leave core.
  • Probe (POST /api/capabilities/mcp/:id/probe) is explicit-request-only, against enabled+trusted servers: a real MCP initialize + tools/list with a 15s hard timeout. Failure → 502 + probe_status='failed' — it never disables the server.
  • Token estimates are estimates: ceil(chars/4), ±25% (core/src/token-estimate.ts is the one swap seam) — relative UX signals, never billed figures. An MCP server's estimate is null until probed.

Local-model agent runtime (D-072)

Ollama-routed runs execute K's in-process tool loop by default (§02). Its knobs:

Var / config key Default Meaning
K_OLLAMA_AGENT_MODE (unset) legacy reverts ollama runs to the old ollama run prompt pipe (no tools/MCP)
K_OLLAMA_FS_SCOPE (worktree) any widens the native tools' fs guard from the run worktree to the whole filesystem
K_OLLAMA_NUM_CTX → ollama.numCtx 16384 the explicit num_ctx sent per chat call; the env var is the first-run seed for the app_config key ollama.numCtx (runtime-editable, seed-then-override like the other runtime config)

Built-in limits (constants, not env): 25 iterations, 10-minute run timeout, 60s per MCP tool call, a repeated-identical-call detector, and a context-pressure warning above 85% of the model's shown context length.

Remote access (exposing K beyond localhost)

K defaults to a loopback-only posture (HOST=127.0.0.1). Exposing it on a network requires an authentication layer in front of it — never expose the raw port to the open internet.

Token resolution (every boot). The harness bearer token is resolved in this order:

  1. HARNESS_TOKEN env, if set and non-empty (operator override).
  2. A persisted token at data/auth-token (gitignored, 0600 where the FS honours it), if present.
  3. Otherwise a first run: a cryptographically strong token (crypto.randomBytes(32), base64url) is generated, persisted to data/auth-token, and printed once in a prominent setup banner. Copy it then — subsequent boots only print a masked confirmation (last 4 chars) and the file path, never the full token.

There is no insecure hard-coded production default. (pnpm dev seeds the well-known dev-token-change-me via core/dev-env.mjs purely for loopback ergonomics — it is never on the pnpm start / production path.)

Safety gate. If HOST is non-loopback (anything but 127.0.0.1 / ::1 / localhost) and the effective token is empty or the legacy weak literal, core refuses to start with an actionable error. Fix it by setting a strong HARNESS_TOKEN (e.g. openssl rand -base64 32) or by removing the override so a strong token is generated.

Recommended exposure. Put an authenticating layer in front and keep core on loopback wherever possible:

  • Tailscale (preferred). Join the host to your tailnet and reach K at its Tailscale IP/MagicDNS name; leave HOST=127.0.0.1 and bind Tailscale's userspace proxy, or set HOST to the tailnet interface. Access is then gated by your tailnet ACLs and the harness token.
  • Authenticating HTTPS reverse proxy (Caddy/nginx/Cloudflare Access) doing TLS termination + its own auth, proxying to loopback core. Only then is HOST=0.0.0.0 acceptable, and only bound to the proxy's interface.

Dashboard auth. The browser can't send an Authorization header on the WS upgrade, so the main /ws event gateway authenticates a ?token= query param (constant-time compared) and closes unauthenticated sockets with code 4401 before subscribing them to the event bus. On loopback dev the dashboard uses the dev token transparently (no login). Accessed remotely, a REST 401 triggers a login screen: paste the harness token; it is stored in sessionStorage and attached to subsequent REST (Authorization: Bearer …) and WS (?token=…) calls. The real token is never baked into the built bundle, and since W10a (D-066) even the loopback-only dev terminal/harness tokens are emitted only under vite serve — a prod build ships none.

Security hardening — terminal + host credentials (W10a, D-066)

Three defense-in-depth hardenings on top of the loopback default:

  • Scrubbed terminal env (F-084). The browser terminal's node-pty no longer inherits process.env. It runs a scrubbed env — a denylist strips vendor prefixes and any *_TOKEN / *_KEY / *_SECRET / *_PASSWORD plus connection-string *_URL / *_URI / *_DSN / *_PASS / *_PWD / CONNECTION vars (while PATH / HOME / PWD and the like survive) — so even a leaked/guessed terminal token can't echo $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.
  • No dev tokens in a prod bundle (F-084). VITE_TERMINAL_TOKEN and VITE_HARNESS_TOKEN are emitted undefined in a production build (the dev literal is injected only under vite serve), and TerminalPage's token fallback is dev-gated — so pnpm build never ships dev-terminal-token / dev-token-change-me; prod fails closed instead.
  • Remote terminal is opt-in (F-084). A non-loopback HOST with ENABLE_TERMINAL now refuses to boot unless TERMINAL_ALLOW_REMOTE=true — even with a strong TERMINAL_TOKEN (previously only a weak/empty token was refused) — so an accidental LAN bind can't expose a shell.

Credential posture is surfaced + opt-out-able (F-064/F-090). The agent-engine boundary (§02) uses a K-token-first auth with a guarded host-credential dogfooding fallback (D-027). That fallback is now visible: credentialPosture() reports one of managed (a managed ANTHROPIC_API_KEY / CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN is set) · host-fallback (no managed token, fallback ON — runs copy host creds) · disabled, surfaced on /api/status, the Settings auth card, and a one-line boot summary (never the secret itself), so silent host-credential use is no longer invisible. Setting K_DISABLE_HOST_CREDENTIAL_FALLBACK=true opts a deployment out — a run with no managed token is left unauthenticated rather than silently copying host credentials. The default (no flag) is unchanged — OOTB dogfooding is byte-identical; the fallback copy stays chmod 0600 under the gitignored data dir.

Troubleshooting

  • Stale worktree after a crash. A crashed or killed run can leave a .worktrees/<runId> directory behind. On boot, reconcileOnBoot (core/src/supervisor.ts, wired in index.ts) auto-reconciles runs stuck in running/queued to interrupted, removes orphaned .worktrees/* dirs, then runs git worktree prune — the prune runs after dir removal (W10b/F-091) so a crash-orphaned worktree's metadata is reclaimed the same boot. On Windows, removeWorktree retries an EBUSY (3× backoff) before a best-effort swallow (the happy path adds no delay); a file lock that still blocks removal is logged non-fatal — if a stale dir persists, close any process holding it and run git worktree prune manually, then delete the directory.
  • SQLite "database is locked". data/k.db runs in WAL mode. A first boot after a new schema migration, or two core processes pointing at the same DB, can race and surface a lock. Run a single core process, and start the dev server stopped for the first boot after a migration (see Schema migrations). The -wal/-shm sidecar files are normal and are checkpointed automatically.
  • Port already in use. The core binds PORT (default 3001) and the web dev server binds 5173. If either is taken (EADDRINUSE), stop the other process or change PORT in core/.env (and CORS_ORIGIN to match the web origin). On Windows, find the holder with netstat -ano | findstr :3001.
  • A registered project's folder was moved/deleted. The GitHub poller skips a project whose localPath no longer exists instead of erroring every cycle (one warn per boot per project; the row is never deleted or mutated), and the read surface stamps an additive Project.pathMissing so the UI can say why (P5.7, D-058). Re-register or restore the path to resume polling.

HTTP API surface (projects + verification + runs)

Beyond the registry/metrics endpoints, the project + verification surface is:

Endpoint Purpose
POST /api/projects/:id/onboard scaffold the three §05 invariants (starter bible + CI) for whatever is missing; idempotent
POST /api/projects/:id/verify deterministic single-shot verification → VerificationReport. Optional body { deep?: boolean }: deep: true also fire-and-forgets the Layer-2 verify-project agent run (the deterministic report is always returned immediately)
GET /api/projects/:id/verifications report history, newest first
GET /api/runs/:id/events/:seq/raw lazy per-event raw stream-json (404 on missing/null-raw seq, 400 on non-numeric)
GET /api/runs?status=&limit= server-side run filters (status validated; limit 1–500, default 100)
GET /api/profiles[/:id] read-only (W2, F-020) — exposes K / Chief / leads for inspection with no write path, so the authority ceiling stays intact

API robustness (W2, F-015..F-028/F-074). The whole surface now shares one error envelope — error is always a string (sendError / sendZodError), fixing the UI's [object Object] on a validation error — and a consistent validate-body (400) → then existence (404) ordering, so kill / events on a nonexistent run 404 (were 200) and a validation rejection names the offending field. GET /api/chief/org includes the K node above the Chief (F-023 — the full user → K → Chief → leads tree), and workflow_runs gained a workflow_id FK (guarded ALTER, SCHEMA_VERSION 3→4) so a run is traceable to the definition it ran.

artifacts/bible/ this document's source

Schema migrations

db.ts uses CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS for fresh installs, plus an exported, idempotent migrate(d) run at every boot for existing DBs. New columns are added with guarded ALTERs — a pragma table_info check (hasColumn) before each ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN, so re-running is a no-op. Indexes that depend on a migrated column are created inside migrate() (after the ALTER), never in the main DDL block. Migrated DBs append columns at the end, so column order can differ from a fresh install — always reference columns by name. migrate(d) is exported so tests can run it against an old-schema temp DB. First boot after a new migration: run with the dev server stopped to avoid a concurrent-migration race.

The user_version fast path (P5.7, D-059). migrate() runs on EVERY connection open — the main boot AND each per-run stdio MCP child (up to 3 per K/Chief turn) — so the full guarded-ALTER/backfill scan is now version-gated: PRAGMA user_version === SCHEMA_VERSION → return immediately; otherwise run the full scan (migrateSlow) and stamp the version only on success, so a failed migration retries on the next open. Bump discipline (documented at the constant in db.ts): adding any new migration to migrateSlow() requires bumping SCHEMA_VERSION — an older-stamped DB then re-runs the scan and is re-stamped. The per-step guards stay as belt-and-suspenders for the slow path and for concurrent first-boots racing before the stamp lands.

Sentinel guard against a poisoned stamp (F1). The fast path above trusts PRAGMA user_version blindly, which only holds if the stamp and the actual schema agree. migrate() now checks a sentinel column (SCHEMA_SENTINEL = { table: 'projects', column: 'budget_daily_usd' }, updated alongside every SCHEMA_VERSION bump) before honoring a matching stamp: if the sentinel column doesn't actually exist on projects, the stamp is treated as poisoned — migrate() logs a warning and falls through to the full migrateSlow() rescan regardless of what user_version says. Because that rescan is idempotent (guarded ALTERs), it self-heals every missing column and re-stamps correctly with no manual intervention.

The 2026-07-13 poisoned-stamp incident (motivating F1). The dev DB (data/k.db, repo root) was found stamped user_version = 12 with zero of the v12 columns present. Mechanism: a pnpm --filter @k/core dev:watch process was running against the real DB during schema editing; an intermediate save hot-restarted core mid-edit and stamped 12 before the v12 migration block existed in migrateSlow(). The pre-F1 fast path trusted that stamp unconditionally, so migrate() returned immediately on every later boot and the DB was never repaired. After the P5 merge landed, module-level db.prepare() statements referencing v12 columns crashed core at import (SqliteError: no such column: budget_daily_usd) → crash-loop → every /api route 503 — the whole backend appeared dead. CI, all test suites, and live smokes all exercise fresh DBs (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS), so none of them could have caught an upgrade-path bug like this — see pnpm smoke:upgrade under Dev-stack hygiene, which now closes that gap.

Recovery procedure for a DB found in this state: stop every process pointed at it, force the stamp back to zero (PRAGMA user_version = 0), then boot core once with the rest of the stack stopped — the migration scan runs unconditionally at user_version = 0, and being idempotent, heals every missing column and re-stamps to the current SCHEMA_VERSION. With F1 in place this manual recovery is now also the automatic behavior the next time a poisoned stamp is opened.

One-shot, flag-guarded migrations (P5.7). Some evolutions cannot be plain-idempotent ALTERs and run exactly once, guarded by an app_config flag (re-checked inside an IMMEDIATE transaction, so racing processes no-op):

  • mig_work_items_run_scope (D-053) — a table REBUILD of work_items (SQLite cannot alter a CHECK in place: copy → drop → rename with FKs toggled off) onto the final run|personal|org|project scope enum, re-stamping legacy personal/org rows to 'run'.
  • mig_project_tasks_drop (D-058) — the final project_tasks → work_items backfill followed by DROP TABLE project_tasks (fixes the boot-resurrection of API-deleted tasks); a partial UNIQUE (project_id, issue_number) index (after a dedupe) makes issue-mirroring idempotent.
  • migration.agentProfileModelReset.v1 (D-056) — un-freezes profile rows still pinning the historical 'claude-sonnet-4-6' literal to the '' runtime-default sentinel.
  • mig_agent_memory_profile_backfill (D-053) — best-effort backfill of agent_memory.profile_id from each lesson's source-run activation.

verification_reports.score_breakdown (TEXT, JSON) was added via one of these guarded idempotent ALTERs — it stores the four §07 score components (ci, coverage, bible, findings) so the Verification tab can render per-weight bars without recomputing. Older reports without the column still validate (the breakdown is optional on read).

verification_reports.coverage_pct (REAL) was added the same way (F4) — it stores the project's measured overall line-coverage % at verify time. runVerification reads the previous report's coverage_pct to derive the live coverage trend (§07): a coverage file present + below the prior reading → a real score penalty; no coverage file → unknown → neutral. NULL for older reports / uninstrumented projects; the field is nullable/optional on read.

Continuous integration

.github/workflows/ci.yml runs on push to main/feat/**/fix/** and PRs to main (concurrency-cancel, contents: read). Steps on ubuntu-latest / Node 20: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile → pnpm typecheck → pnpm -r test → pnpm build. Root package.json pins packageManager: pnpm@<version> so pnpm/action-setup matches the local toolchain. better-sqlite3 uses Node-20 prebuilds. gh CLI is absent locally — check CI status via the GitHub web UI or the REST API with a git credential token.

Accepted risks

  • /health is auth-exempt by design (a liveness probe; no sensitive data).
  • The main /ws event gateway is authenticated (Phase 4): exempt from the header hook (a browser WS can't send one) but its handler validates a ?token= query param with a constant-time compare and closes unauthenticated sockets with code 4401 before subscribing them. /ws/terminal keeps its own scoped TERMINAL_TOKEN gate (default-off feature).
  • Setting HOST=0.0.0.0 is still only safe behind Tailscale or an authenticating HTTPS reverse proxy (see Remote access). The non-loopback safety gate prevents booting that posture with a weak/empty token.

Phase 5 — Agent Organization (storage / env / key files)

The agent org's storage is BUILT end to end — P5.0 laid the foundation (agent_profiles + agent_runs, authority.ts, startAgentRun, D-037) and the later waves filled in every row below (the last: the unified work_items finalization + project_tasks drop, P5.7). All tables follow the migration discipline above (guarded ALTERs, one-shot flags where a rebuild/drop was required).

Tables.

Table Status Holds
agent_profiles BUILT (P5.0) one row per durable profile (K · Chief · orchestrator + the five leads): tier, charter (charter-asset basename), default_model, allowed_tools (JSON), mcp_servers (JSON), skills (JSON); name UNIQUE so the seed is idempotent
agent_memory BUILT (kstore; profile_id added P5.0) gated lessons (layer A): run_id (source run), lesson, status (pending/accepted/rejected), created_at, reviewed_at, + profile_id (P5.0) — the gated-reflection store
agent_runs BUILT (P5.0) one activation of a profile into a supervised run: profile_id, run_id, trigger, goal, project_id, workflow_id, status — the startAgentRun tracking row (rides the run-lifecycle seam)
workflow_definitions BUILT (P5.3b) named workflows (NamedWorkflow): name, roles (JSON), prompt_scaffold, cross_project — the generalization of the single buildDelegationPrompt (D-047)
work_items BUILT (unified — D-045/D-048/D-053/D-058) THE one scoped task store: scope ∈ run|personal|org|project (run = ephemeral kstore default; personal/org durable operator-global; project carries project_id + issue-sync columns). project_tasks is dropped (one-shot mig_project_tasks_drop)
lead_dispatches BUILT (P5.6 loop-b) the Chief→lead dispatch intent queue (pending/dispatched/failed) drained by the main-process relay; completed intents retire by liveness derivation (D-060)

Org env / runtime config (as-built). The kstore/logistics/mgmt MCP servers need no env — they are stdio children whose command/env the synthesizer rewrites per run. The org loop's toggles: CHIEF_WAKE=0 (disable the Chief wake), CHIEF_WAKE_CRON (the schedule tick), LEAD_DISPATCH_RELAY=0 (disable the relay), plus two app_config keys governing event wakes (P5.7, D-057): chief_wake_max_per_hour (rolling-hour cap, default 6) and chief_wake_events_enabled (kill switch, '1' default, hot-flippable). Still planned: the Google connector credentials K would mount (Calendar/Gmail/Drive — connectors not wired, §03) and a flag for K's hybrid idle-timeout.
